\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}+\sup_{t,j}\lVert\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{1})}_{t,j}-\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{2})}_{t,j}\rVert

for some constant
C_{\Delta}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result. Moreover, for any
t,j
, we have

\lVert\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{1})}_{t,j}-\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{2})}_{t,j}\rVert\leq\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\lVert G(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)},t)\rVert\leq\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}\int_{s_{1}}^{s_{2}}\lVert G(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)},t)\rVert^{2}\lesssim(1+\lambda^{2})\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}},

(D.8)

where the universal boundedness of
\lVert G(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)},t)-\lambda\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j}\rVert
can be readily derived from the third result of Lemma
C.3
alongside Assumption
2
(ii). Therefore, we obtain

\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{2})}_{t,j},\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s_{2})},t)-G(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{2})}_{t,j},\rho^{(s_{2})},t)\right\rVert-\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{1})}_{t,j},\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s_{1})},t)-G(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{1})}_{t,j},\rho^{(s_{1})},t)\right\rVert\lesssim(1+\lambda+\lambda^{2}+\lambda^{3})\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}

(D.9)

for any
s_{1},s_{2}\in[0,\tau]
.

Define
\tau_{n}=n\tau/L^{2}
for
n=1,2,\dots,L^{2}
. Leveraging Lemma
D.3
and applying the union bound over
n\in[L^{2}]
(updating the
\delta
in the lemma to
\delta+2\log L
), we obtain, with a probability of at least
1-\exp(-\delta)
with respect to the parameter initialization
\Theta^{(0)}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\{\tau_{n}\}_{n\in[L^{2}]}}\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)},t)-G(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\rho^{(s)},t)\right\rVert\lesssim

\displaystyle(1+\lambda+\lambda^{2}+\lambda^{3})L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log L+\log(L+1)}{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}

Furthermore, leveraging (
D.9
), we deduce

\displaystyle\sup_{t,j}\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)},t)-G(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\rho^{(s)},t)\right\rVert\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{t,j}\sup_{s\in\{\tau_{n}\}_{n\in[L^{2}]}}\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)},t)-G(\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j},\rho^{(s)},t)\right\rVert

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq\tau/L^{2}}(1+\lambda+\lambda^{2}+\lambda^{3})\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

Returning to (
D.6
), we establish that with a probability of at least
1-\exp(-\delta)
with respect to the parameter initialization
\Theta^{(0)}
,

\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\sup_{t,j}\lVert\beta^{(s)}_{t,j}-\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j}\rVert=\Delta(\tau)\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

We denote the event where the above inequality holds as
E_{1}
, thus we have
\mathbb{P}(E_{1})\geq 1-\exp(-\delta)
.

Step III: Prove the finite time results

Now, we are poised to demonstrate the results in Theorem
3.1
that concern supremacy over
s\in[0,\tau]
. The verification of Lemmas
C.4
reveals the existence of a universal constant
B_{\tau}:=B\exp(K(1+R_{\tau}+R_{\tau}^{2}))
such that

\max\{\lVert T_{\rho^{(\tau)}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(\tau)}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(\tau)}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\}\leq B_{\tau}

for any
H
and
t\in[0,1]
.

Utilizing Lemma
D.6
and applying the union bound over
n\in[L^{2}]
, we observe

\sup_{s\in\{\tau_{n}\}_{n\in[L^{2}]}}\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)}}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{(s)}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

Additionally, note that for any
s_{1},s_{2}\in[0,\tau]
, we derive from Lemmas
C.2
and
C.5
that

\displaystyle\Big|\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s_{1})}}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{(s_{1})}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}-\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s_{2})}}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{(s_{2})}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\Big|

(D.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s_{1})}}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s_{2})}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}+\lVert T_{\rho^{(s_{1})}}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{(s_{2})}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{t,j}\lVert\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{1})}_{t,j}-\widetilde{\beta}^{(s_{2})}_{t,j}\rVert+W_{1}(\rho^{(s_{1})},\rho^{(s_{2})})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}

where the last inequality is derived utilizing (
D.3
) and (
D.8
). Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)}}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{(s)}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in\{\tau_{n}\}_{n\in[L^{2}]}}\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)}}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{(s)}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}+\sup_{|s_{2}-s_{1}|\leq\tau/L^{2}}\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}

(D.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}

with probability at by
1-\exp(-\delta)
. We denote the event where the above inequality holds as
E_{2}
, thus we have
\mathbb{P}(E_{2})\geq 1-\exp(-\delta)
. Considering that
\{\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j}\}_{t,j}
could be regarded as i.i.d. samples drawn from
\{\rho^{(s)}\}_{t,j}
, employing a similar method with the concentration guarantee from Lemma
C.7
, we can readily deduce the existence of an event
E_{3}
with
\mathbb{P}(E_{3})\geq 1-\exp(-\delta)
such that, under
E_{3}
, we have

\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j}\rVert^{2}-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho^{(s)}(\beta,t)d\beta dt|\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

Now, let’s analyze the scenario under the probability event
E_{1}\cap E_{2}\cap E_{3}
with
\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cap E_{2}\cap E_{3})\geq 1-3\exp(-\delta)
. Lemma
C.5
demonstrates that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(s)}}(H,t)]-\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)}}(H,t)]|\leq\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(s)}}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

(D.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\sup_{t,j}\lVert\beta^{(s)}_{t,j}-\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j}\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

This further implies, by (
D.11
), that

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(s)}}(H,t)]-\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho^{(s)}}(H,t)]|

(D.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(s)}}(H,t)]-\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)}}(H,t)]|+\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(s)}}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{(s)}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}},

Since
\max\{\lVert T_{\rho^{(\tau)}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(\tau)}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}^{(\tau)}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\}
is universally bounded, (
D.13
) immediately indicates that

\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\widehat{R}(\Theta^{(s)})-R(\rho^{(s)})|\lesssim\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(s)}}(H,t)]-\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho^{(s)}}(H,t)]|\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}},

(D.14)

and

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(s)})-Q(\rho^{(s)})|\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\widehat{R}(\Theta^{(s)})-R(\rho^{(s)})|+\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}|\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\widetilde{\beta}^{(s)}_{t,j}\rVert^{2}-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho^{(s)}(\beta,t)d\beta dt|

(D.15)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

Step IV: Prove the weakly convergence

For the remainder of the proof, we adopt a similar approach as in the proof of Theorem 2.6 in
[
16
]
.
We denote
\hat{\rho}
as
\hat{\rho}_{M,L}
for any given
M
and
L
. It’s essential to note that we treat
\hat{\rho}_{M,L}
as probability measures in this step of the proof.

Recalling (
D.3
), for any
s_{1},s_{2}\in[0,\tau]
, we have

W_{2}(\hat{\rho}_{M,L}^{(s_{1})},\hat{\rho}_{M,L}^{(s_{2})})\leq C(1+\lambda)\sqrt{s_{2}-s_{1}}

for some constant
C
dependent on the parameters listed in the result. We observe that the family of curves
(s\mapsto\hat{\rho}_{M,L}^{(s)})_{M,L}
is equicontinuous in
W_{2}
on
[0,\tau]
, uniformly in
M,L
. Additionally, the family
(\hat{\rho}_{M,L})_{M,L}
lies within a
W_{2}
ball, thus weakly precompact. As the weak topology is weaker than the topology induced by
W_{2}
, according to the Arzelà–Ascoli theorem, along any sequence where
L\rightarrow\infty
and
\log L/M\rightarrow\infty
, we can identify a subsequence that converges weakly to a certain process
(\nu^{(s)})_{s\geq 0}\in\pazocal{P}^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, concentrated on
P_{R_{\tau}}
at all times. In the subsequent analysis, we solely focus on this subsequence, still denoted as
(\hat{\rho}_{M,L})_{M,L}
.

For any
t\in[0,1]
, let’s define the sequence
(E^{\top}_{M,L})_{M,L}
of momentum fields, which is a vector-valued measure on
[0,\tau]\times\Omega
, denoted by
E_{M,L}:=\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta_{M,L}^{(s)},t)\hat{\rho}^{(s)}(\beta,t)ds
. We also define
E:=G(\beta,\nu^{(s)},t)\nu^{(s)}(\beta,t)ds
.

Considering that both
\hat{\rho}_{M,L}
and
\nu
are concentrated on
P_{R_{\tau}}
, we also have uniform convergence in the Bounded Lipschitz metric. Hence, for any bounded and Lipschitz function
\varphi:[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}\beta}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}\beta}
, it holds

\lVert\hat{\rho}^{(s)}-\nu^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{BL}}\rightarrow 0

uniformly among
s\in[0,\tau]
along the sequence.

Note that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\varphi\cdot d(E_{M,L}-E)\Big|\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\max}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta_{M,L}^{(s)},t)-G(\beta,\nu^{(s)},t)\right\rVert\hat{\rho}^{(s)}(\beta,t)d\beta dtds

(D.16)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\varphi\cdot(\hat{\rho}^{(s)}-\nu^{s})(\beta,t)d\beta dtds\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\max}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta_{M,L}^{(s)},t)-G(\beta,\hat{\rho}_{M,L}^{(s)},t)\right\rVert\hat{\rho}^{(s)}(\beta,t)d\beta dtds

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\max}\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert G(\beta,\hat{\rho}^{(s)},t)-G(\beta,\nu^{(s)},t)\right\rVert\hat{\rho}^{(s)}(\beta,t)d\beta dtds

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\lVert\hat{\rho}^{(s)}-\nu^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{BL}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\lVert\hat{\rho}^{(s)}-\nu^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{BL}}+\sup_{s\in[0,\tau],\lVert\beta\rVert\leq R_{\tau}}\left\lVert G(\beta,\hat{\rho}^{(s)},t)-G(\beta,\nu^{(s)},t)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}+\sup_{s\in[0,\tau]}\lVert\hat{\rho}^{(s)}-\nu^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{BL}}

for some constant
C_{E}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result and with probability at least
1-\exp(-\delta)
with respect to the parameter initialization
\Theta^{(0)}
for any
\delta>0
.
Here, the third inequality of (
D.16
) utilizes the third result of Lemma
D.3
, and the fourth inequality uses the second result of Lemma
D.3
, following a similar process in Step II to achieve supremacy over
s\in[0,\tau]
.

From (
D.16
), we infer that
\Big|\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\varphi\cdot d(E_{M,L}-E)\Big|\rightarrow 0
almost surely along the sequence. Hence,
E_{M,L}
converges weakly to
E
almost surely along the sequence, and the particle gradient flow for
(\nu^{(\tau)})_{\tau\geq 0}
almost surely satisfies (
2.7
) on
[0,\tau]
for any arbitrarily given
\tau>0
. According to the Fokker-Planck equation without noise involved
[
59
]
, we conclude that
(\nu^{(\tau)})_{\tau\geq 0}
almost surely satisfies (
3.5
). Consequently, the uniqueness stated in Proposition
3.2
ensures that
(\nu^{(\tau)})_{\tau\geq 0}=(\rho^{(\tau)})_{\tau\geq 0}
almost surely.
∎

D.2
Proof of Proposition
3.1

Proof.

Suppose that the Fréchet derivative
\frac{\delta R}{\delta\rho}
indeed exists, we establish

\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}(\theta,w,t)=\frac{\delta R}{\delta\rho}(\theta,w,t)+\frac{\lambda}{2}(\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lVert w\rVert_{2}^{2}).

Therefore, it suffices to show that the Fréchet derivative of
L
with respect to
\rho
is

\frac{\delta R}{\delta\rho}(\beta,t)=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\mathrm{Tr}\Big(g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)^{\top}p_{\rho}(H,t)\Big)\Big].

(D.17)

Denote
\rho_{\eta}=\rho+\eta(\nu-\rho)
. We provide the following lemma to bound
T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,1)-T_{\rho}(H,1)
by expanding the first-order derivative as follows

Lemma D.4

(First-order derivative of Transformer output)
.

Under Assumption
2
, for any
H
and
\rho,\nu\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
that have bounded supports, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{vec}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,1)-T_{\rho}(H,1)]=

\displaystyle\eta\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta)\Big)

(D.18)

\displaystyle\cdot\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)](\nu-\rho)(\beta,t)d\beta dt+o(\eta),

where
\rho_{\eta}:=\rho+\eta(\nu-\rho)
.

Given (
D.18
), we observe from the solution of
p_{\rho}
(
C.13
) and (
C.14
) that

\displaystyle\Big(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,1)-T_{\rho}(H,1)]

(D.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{vec}[p_{\rho}(H,1)]^{\top}\mathrm{vec}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,1)-T_{\rho}(H,1)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\mathrm{vec}[p_{\rho}(H,t)]^{\top}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)](\rho-\nu)(\beta,t)d\beta dt+o(\eta)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)^{\top}p_{\rho}(H,t)\Big)(\rho-\nu)(\beta,t)d\beta dt+o(\eta),

Hence, by applying (
D.19
) to the risk function, we obtain

\displaystyle R(\rho_{\eta})-R(\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\Big(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)^{2}-\Big(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\Big(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,1)]\Big]

\displaystyle+\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,1)]O(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,1)-T_{\rho}(H,1)])

\displaystyle=\eta\Big\langle\frac{\delta R}{\delta\rho},\nu-\rho\Big\rangle+o(\eta),

which indicates (
D.17
) and concludes the proof.
∎

D.3
Proof of well-posedness of Wasserstein gradient flow

Proof.

Following a similar idea as Proposition 2.5 of
[
16
]
, we leverage the general theory of Wasserstein gradient flow developed in
[
4
]
.
Define the functional family
Q_{r}(\rho)
as

Q_{r}(\rho)=\begin{cases}Q(\rho)\quad&\rho(P_{r})=1,\\
\infty\quad&\mathrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

For any
r>0
, let’s consider any
admissible transport

\gamma\in\pazocal{P}^{\Omega\times\Omega}
concentrated on
P_{r}
. By definition, both of its marginals, denoted by
\rho_{1}
and
\rho_{2}
, are concentrated on
P_{r}
. We define the transport cost for
\gamma
as

C_{p}(\gamma):=(\int|x-y|^{p}d\gamma(x,y))^{1/p}

for
p\geq 1
. Additionally, we denote the transport interpolation as
\rho_{\alpha}^{\gamma}:=((1-\alpha)\rho_{1}+\alpha\rho_{2})_{\#\gamma}
. Our proof consists of several steps outlined below.

Step I: Show that
Q_{r}
is proper and continuous for
W_{2}
on its closed domain:

Note that the parameters
r,D,N,\lambda
remain fixed throughout this proof step, so we hide the constant dependencies on them. For any
(\beta,t)\in P_{r}
, we have
Q_{r}(\delta_{(\beta,t)})=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\mathrm{Read}[H+\Delta tg(H,\beta)]-y(H))^{2}]+\frac{\lambda}{2}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}<\infty
. This indicates that
Q_{r}
is proper. Moreover, for any
\rho,\nu\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
whose bounded support belong to
P_{r}
, Lemma
C.1
ensures that

\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)+T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}=O(1),

and Lemma
C.2
guarantees that

\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}=O(W_{1}(\rho,\nu))=O(W_{2}(\rho,\nu))

for any
H
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle R(\nu)-R(\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\Big(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\nu}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)^{2}-\Big(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)^{2}\Big]

(D.20)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\Big(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\nu}(H,1)]\Big]

\displaystyle+\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\nu}(H,1)]O(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\nu}(H,1)-T_{\rho}(H,1)])

\displaystyle=O(W_{2}(\rho,\nu)).

Furthermore, since both
\rho
and
\nu
have bounded support,
\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}
is Lipschitz continuous with respect to
(\beta,t)
. Therefore, by the Kantorovich-Rubinstein Theorem (see Theorem 5.10 of
[
68
]
, for example), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}(\rho-\nu)d\beta dt\Big|=O(W_{1}(\rho,\nu))=O(W_{2}(\rho,\nu)).

(D.21)

Combining (
D.20
) and (
D.21
), we obtain that
Q(\rho)-Q(\nu)=O(W_{2}(\rho,\nu)).
Therefore,
Q_{r}
is continuous for
W_{2}
on its closed domain.

Step II: Show that
\alpha\mapsto Q(\rho_{\alpha}^{\gamma})/C_{2}^{2}(\gamma)
is differentiable and has a Lipschitz continuous derivative

Let’s denote
h(\alpha):=Q_{r}(\rho_{\alpha}^{\gamma})
.
Lemma
C.3
ensures that for any
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
with bounded support belonging to
P_{r}
, we have bounded
\lVert\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}(\cdot,\cdot)\rVert_{F}
on
P_{r}
. Therefore,
Q_{r}(\rho_{\alpha}^{\gamma})
is differentiable with respect to
t
, and the derivative reads

\displaystyle h^{\prime}(\alpha)=

\displaystyle\langle\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho=\rho_{\alpha}^{\gamma}},\frac{d}{d\alpha}\rho_{\alpha}^{\gamma}\rangle

(D.22)

\displaystyle=\int d\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho=\rho_{\alpha}^{\gamma}}\Big((1-\alpha)(\beta_{1},t_{1})+\alpha(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big]\Big[(\beta_{1},t_{1})-(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big)d\gamma((\beta_{1},t_{1}),(\beta_{2},t_{2}))\Big\}.

Then, it suffices to show that
h^{\prime}(\alpha)
is Lipschitz continuous. To accomplish this, we first propose the following lemma for later use:

Lemma D.5

(Locally Lipschitz of
\rho
for the gradient)
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, for any
\rho,\nu\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
concentrated on
P_{r}
, there exists some constant
L_{r}
depending on
r,N,D
and parameters of the assumptions such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)-p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq L_{r}\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1},

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,t)\in P_{r}}\Big|\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho}(\beta,t)-\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\nu}(\beta,t)\Big|\leq L_{r}\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}.

Returning to the lemma proof, for
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in[0,1]
, by the triangle inequality we have
|h^{\prime}(\alpha_{1})-h^{\prime}(\alpha_{2})|\leq J_{1}+J_{2}
where

\displaystyle J_{1}:=

\displaystyle\Big|\int d\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho=\rho_{\alpha_{1}}^{\gamma}}\Big((1-\alpha_{1})(\beta_{1},t_{1})+\alpha_{1}(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big)\Big[(\beta_{1},t_{1})-(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big]d\gamma((\beta_{1},t_{1}),(\beta_{2},t_{2}))

(D.23)

\displaystyle-\int d\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho=\rho_{\alpha_{2}}^{\gamma}}\Big((1-\alpha_{1})(\beta_{1},t_{1})+\alpha_{1}(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big)\Big[(\beta_{1},t_{1})-(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big]d\gamma((\beta_{1},t_{1}),(\beta_{2},t_{2}))\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,t)\in P_{r}}\Big|\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho}(\beta,t)-\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\nu}(\beta,t)\Big|\int{\lVert(\beta_{1},t_{1})-(\beta_{2},t_{2})\rVert_{1}}d\gamma((\beta_{1},t_{1}),(\beta_{2},t_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{r}\lVert\rho_{\alpha_{1}}^{\gamma}-\rho_{\alpha_{2}}^{\gamma}\rVert_{1}\cdot C_{1}(\gamma)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{r}C_{1}^{2}(\gamma)|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{r}C_{2}^{2}(\gamma)|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|

by Lemma
D.5
. The final inequality of (
D.23
) applies Hölder’s inequality to obtain that
C_{1}^{2}(\gamma)\leq C_{2}^{2}(\gamma)
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle J_{2}:=

\displaystyle\Big|\int d\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho=\rho_{\alpha_{2}}^{\gamma}}\Big\{\Big[(1-\alpha_{1})(\beta_{1},t_{1})+\alpha_{1}(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big]\Big[(\beta_{1},t_{1})-(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big]d\gamma((\beta_{1},t_{1}),(\beta_{2},t_{2}))\Big\}

(D.24)

\displaystyle-\int d\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho=\rho_{\alpha_{2}}^{\gamma}}\Big\{\Big[(1-\alpha_{2})(\beta_{1},t_{1})+\alpha_{2}(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big]\Big[(\beta_{1},t_{1})-(\beta_{2},t_{2})\Big]d\gamma((\beta_{1},t_{1}),(\beta_{2},t_{2}))\Big\}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{(\beta,t)\in P_{r}}\left\lVert\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho=\rho_{\alpha_{2}}^{\gamma}}(\beta,t)\right\rVert\Big|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}\Big|\int\lVert(\beta_{1},t_{1})-(\beta_{2},t_{2})\rVert^{2}d\gamma((\beta_{1},t_{1}),(\beta_{2},t_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L^{\prime}_{r}C_{2}^{2}(\gamma)|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}|

where
L^{\prime}_{r}:=\sup_{(\beta,t)\in P_{r}}\lVert\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\big|_{\rho=\rho_{\alpha_{2}}^{\gamma}}(\beta,t)\rVert<\infty
from Lemma
C.3
. Combining (
D.23
) and (
D.24
) leads us to the result that
h^{\prime}(\alpha)/C_{2}^{2}(\gamma)
is Lipschitz continuous.

Step III: Show the well-posedness of Wasserstein gradient flow at some finite time

We follow a similar approach to the proof of Proposition 2.5 in
[
16
]
.
Since
h^{\prime}(\alpha)
is
\lambda_{h}\times C_{2}^{2}(\gamma)
-Lipschitz continuous with respect to
\alpha
for some
\lambda_{h}
, the well-posedness of the Wasserstein gradient flow for
Q_{r}
with the velocity field constrained on
P_{r}
is a corollary of Theorem 11.2.2 of
[
4
]
. Specifically, there exists a unique curve
(\rho_{r}^{(\tau)})_{\tau\geq 0}
continuous in
\pazocal{P}^{2}
such that:

\frac{d\rho_{r}^{(\tau)}}{d\tau}=\mathrm{div}_{\beta}(\rho_{r}^{(\tau)}v_{r}^{(\tau)})

where

v_{r}^{(\tau)}(\beta,t)=\begin{cases}G(\beta,\rho_{r}^{(\tau)},t),\quad&(\beta,t)\in P_{r},\\
0,\quad&\mathrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

for
\rho_{r}^{(\tau)}
-a.e. Given the initialization
\rho_{0}
concentrated on
P_{R}
, for any
r>R
, the unique
\rho_{r}^{(\tau)}
exhibits a first exit time denoted as

\tau_{r}:=\inf\{\tau>0:\rho_{r}^{(\tau)}(P_{r})<1\}.

By defining this exit time, for any
\bar{r}>r
and
\tau\in[0,\tau_{r}]
, we observe
v_{r}(\tau)(\beta,t)=G(\beta,\rho_{r}^{(\tau)},t)
and
v_{\bar{r}}(\tau)(\beta,t)=G(\beta,\rho_{\bar{r}}^{(\tau)},t)
. Due to uniqueness, we infer
\rho_{r}^{(\tau)}=\rho_{\bar{r}}^{(\tau)}
on
\tau\in[0,\tau_{r}]
. Considering
\rho_{r}^{(\tau)}
as the solution to (
3.5
), we establish the existence and uniqueness of the Wasserstein gradient flow for
Q
over
[0,\tau_{r}]
.

Step IV: Show the well-posedness of Wasserstein gradient flow at all time

To establish the Wasserstein gradient flow’s definition for
\tau\geq 0
, it’s necessary to demonstrate that
\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\tau_{r}=\infty
. For any
r>R
, according to the energy identity in Theorem 11.2.1 of
[
4
]
, on
[0,t_{r}]
, we observe that
\tau\mapsto Q(\rho^{(\tau)})
is non-increasing. Specifically, this represents

\displaystyle\frac{dQ(\rho^{(\tau)})}{d\tau}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\Big\langle\frac{dQ}{d\rho}\bigg|_{\rho=\rho^{(\tau)}},\mathrm{div}_{\beta}(\rho^{(\tau)}G(\beta,\rho^{(\tau)},t))\Big\rangle

(D.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\Big\langle G(\beta,\rho^{(\tau)},t),\mathrm{div}_{\beta}(\rho^{(\tau)}G(\beta,\rho^{(\tau)},t))\Big\rangle d\beta dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\rho^{(\tau)}\lVert G(\beta,\rho^{(\tau)},t)\rVert^{2}_{2}d\beta dt\leq 0.

Therefore, for any
\tau\in[0,\tau_{r}]
, utilizing Lemma
C.1
, we have

\displaystyle Q(\rho^{(\tau)})\leq Q(\rho_{0})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\Big(\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho_{0}}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)^{2}\Big]+\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho_{0}(\beta,t)d\beta dt

(D.26)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\lVert T_{\rho_{0}}(H,1)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}+B)^{2}]+\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\lVert T_{\rho_{0}}(H,1)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}^{2}+B^{2}]+\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle B^{2}+B^{2}\exp\Big(K(1+R+R^{2})\Big)^{2}+\frac{\lambda R^{2}}{2}

Thus, we have
\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho^{(\tau)}(\beta,t)\leq\frac{2}{\lambda}Q(\rho^{(\tau)})\leq R^{2}+\lambda^{-1}\Big(2B^{2}+2B^{2}\exp\Big(K(1+R+R^{2})\Big)^{2}\Big)=A_{0}^{2}
. According to Assumption (ii) and Lemma
C.3
, for any
(\beta,t)\in P_{r}
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert v_{r}^{(\tau)}(\beta,t)-\lambda\beta\rVert=\lVert G(\beta,\rho^{(\tau)},t)-\lambda\beta\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\left\lVert\nabla_{\beta}\Big\{g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)_{:,i}\Big\}p_{\rho}(H,t)_{:,i}\right\rVert

(D.27)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\left\lVert\nabla_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)_{:,i}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\left\lVert\nabla_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\phi_{P}(\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}})\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}(1+\lVert\beta\rVert)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{R}(1+\lVert\beta\rVert),

where
C_{R}:=\sqrt{N+1}\phi_{P}(B\exp(K(1+A_{0}+A_{0}^{2})))(B+B\exp(K(1+A_{0}+A_{0}^{2})))\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+A_{0}+A_{0}^{2}))(1+A_{0}+A_{0}^{2})\Big)
. Applying (
D.27
) to the gradient flow equation

\frac{d\beta^{(\tau)}}{d\tau}=-v_{r}^{(\tau)}(\beta,t),\quad\beta^{(0)}=\beta

for
\tau\geq 0
, we obtain
\frac{d\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert}{d\tau}=\frac{\langle-v_{r}^{(\tau)}(\beta,t),\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert\rangle}{\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert}\leq\lVert v_{r}^{(\tau)}(\beta,t)-\lambda\beta\rVert\leq C_{R}(1+\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert)
. This indicates

\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert\leq(\lVert\beta\rVert+1)\exp(C_{R}\tau)-1\leq(R+1)\exp(C_{R}\tau)-1

(D.28)

by the Grönwall’s inequality. Therefore, for any
T>0
,
\rho^{(T)}
is concentrated on
P_{(R+1)\exp(C_{R}T)-1}
, implying that for
r>(R+1)\exp(C_{R}T)
, we have
\tau_{r}>T
. Hence, we conclude
\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\tau_{r}=\infty
, establishing the existence of a unique Wasserstein gradient flow from (
3.5
) over
\tau>0
.

Eventually, we establish the three properties listed in Proposition
3.2
for
\rho^{(\tau)}
. By (
3.5
), for any
t\in[0,1]
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\beta}\rho^{(\tau)}(\beta,t)d\beta=

\displaystyle\int_{\beta}\rho^{(0)}(\beta,t)d\beta+\int_{0}^{\tau}\Big(\int_{\beta}\mathrm{div}_{\beta}(\rho^{(s)}G^{(s)}(\beta,\rho^{(s)},t))d\beta\Big)ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+\int_{0}^{\tau}0\cdot ds=1

indicated by the Divergence Theorem as
\rho^{(s)}
has bounded support.
Next, for any
\tau\geq 0
, (
D.28
) shows that
\rho^{(\tau)}
is concentrated on
P_{R_{\tau}}
. Moreover, (
D.25
) now holds for any
\tau>0
, implying
\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho^{(\tau)}(\beta,t)\leq A_{0}^{2}
for any
\tau\geq 0
.
∎

D.4
Proof of well-posedness of gradient flow

Proposition D.1

(Existence and uniqueness of gradient flow)
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
, for any initialization of
\Theta^{(0)}
i.i.d. drawn from
\{\rho_{0}(\theta,w|t)\}_{t,j}
, there exists a unique solution
(\Theta^{(\tau)})_{\tau\geq 0}
for (
2.7
). Additionally, for any
\tau\geq 0
, we have

i.
\Theta^{(\tau)}
has a bounded support, meaning
\sup_{t,j}(\lVert\theta^{(\tau)}_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lVert w^{(\tau)}_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2})\leq R_{\tau}
.

ii.
\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(\lVert\theta^{(\tau)}_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lVert w^{(\tau)}_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2})\leq A_{0}^{2}
.

Here,
R_{\tau}
and
A_{0}
are defined as in Proposition
3.2
.

Proof.

The local Lipschitz continuity established in Lemma
D.2
directly implies the continuity of
\widehat{G}_{\beta}(\beta_{t,j},\Theta,t)
with respect to
\Theta
. Since
\{ML\cdot\widehat{G}_{\beta}(\beta_{t,j},\Theta,t)\}_{t/\Delta t+1\in[L],j\in[M]}
serves as the gradient of
\widehat{Q}(\Theta)
, it follows that
\widehat{Q}(\Theta)
is continuously differentiable, indicating the local semiconvexity of
\widehat{Q}(\Theta)
. Specifically, for any
\Theta
, there exists some
\kappa>0
such that
\widehat{Q}(\Theta)+\kappa\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2}+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2})
is convex within a small neighborhood of
\Theta
. The existence and uniqueness of a gradient flow over the maximal interval
[0,\tau_{\max}]
is a standard result (see Section 2.1 of
[
61
]
).

For any
\tau\in[0,\tau_{\max}]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(0)})\geq\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(0)})-\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau)})=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\langle\frac{d\widehat{Q}(\Theta)}{d\beta_{t,j}}\bigg|_{\Theta=\Theta^{(\tau)}},ML\frac{d\widehat{Q}(\Theta)}{d\beta_{t,j}}\bigg|_{\Theta=\Theta^{(\tau)}}\rangle d\tau

(D.29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\widehat{G}_{\beta}(\beta^{(\tau)}_{t,j},\Theta^{(\tau)},t)\rVert^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML\tau}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\Big(\int_{0}^{\tau}\lVert\widehat{G}_{\beta}(\beta^{(\tau)}_{t,j},\Theta^{(\tau)},t)\rVert d\tau\Big)^{2},

where the last inequality follows from Jensen’s inequality. (
D.29
) establishes that
\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau)})
is both upper and lower bounded, and
\Theta^{(\tau)}
exhibits a bounded curve length over any the time interval
[0,\tau_{\max}]
. By compactness, if
\tau_{\max}
is finite, then
\Theta^{(\tau_{\max})}
exists and thus must exist beyond
\tau_{\max}
, which leads to contradiction. Therefore,
\tau_{\max}=\infty
, and the well-posedness of the gradient flow for
\tau\geq 0
consequently follows. Additionally, (
D.29
) shows that for any
\tau\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 2\lambda^{-1}\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau)})\leq 2\lambda^{-1}\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(0)})=

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\Big(\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(0)}}(H,1)]-y(H)\Big)^{2}\Big]

(D.30)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta^{(0)}_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(0)}}(H,1)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}+B)^{2}]+R^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\lambda^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[(\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta^{(0)}}(H,1)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}^{2}+B^{2}]+R^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle R^{2}+\lambda^{-1}\Big(2B^{2}+2B^{2}\exp\Big(K(1+R+R^{2})\Big)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{0}^{2}

The last inequality of (
D.30
) follows from Lemma
C.4
, thereby showing that
\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq A_{0}^{2}
for any
\tau\geq 0
.

As the final part of our proof, we demonstrate that the norm of any entry of
\Theta
is bounded at any given time
\tau\geq 0
. Note that

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta^{(\tau)},t)-\lambda\beta\rVert

(D.31)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\left\lVert\nabla_{\theta}\Big\{f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta)_{:,i}\Big\}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t)_{:,i}\right\rVert/2+\left\lVert\nabla_{w}\Big\{h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta/2),w)_{:,i}\Big\}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)_{:,i}\right\rVert/2

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[N+1]}(\left\lVert\nabla_{\theta}f(T_{\rho}(H,t),\theta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)_{:,i}\rVert/2+\left\lVert\nabla_{w}h(T_{\rho}(H,t),w)_{:,i}\right\rVert\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t/2)_{:,i}\rVert/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\Big(\left\lVert\nabla_{\theta}f(T_{\rho}(H,t),\theta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}/2

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{w}h(T_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t/2),w)_{:,i}\right\rVert\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}/2\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\max\{\phi_{P}(\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}),\phi_{P}(\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}})\}

\displaystyle\max\{\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F},\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}\}(1+\lVert\beta\rVert)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{R}(1+\lVert\beta\rVert),

Applying (
D.31
) to the gradient flow

\frac{d\beta^{(\tau)}_{t,j}}{d\tau}=-\widehat{G}(\beta^{(\tau)}_{t,j},\Theta^{(\tau)},t)

for
\tau\geq 0
, we have
\frac{d\lVert\beta_{t,j}^{(\tau)}\rVert}{d\tau}=\frac{\langle-\widehat{G}(\beta^{(\tau)}_{t,j},\Theta^{(\tau)},t),\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert\rangle}{\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert}\leq\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta^{(\tau)}_{t,j},\Theta^{(\tau)},t)-\lambda\beta\rVert\leq C_{R}(1+\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert)
. This indicates

\lVert\beta^{(\tau)}\rVert\leq(\lVert\beta\rVert+1)\exp(C_{R}\tau)-1\leq(R+1)\exp(C_{R}\tau)-1=R_{\tau}.

(D.32)

∎

D.5
Proof of Proposition
3.3

Before commencing the proof, we introduce two proxy Transformer procedures in addition to
\bar{T}_{\Theta}
and
\widetilde{T}_{\rho}
. The first proxy, denoted as
\bar{T}_{\Theta}
, involves moving the layers with the encoder
h
slightly forward by
\Delta t/2
in the depth index. This adjustment results in a discrete Transformer with only
L
layers, where each layer has a step size of
\Delta t
and an encoder of
(f+h)/2
, represented by
g
. Specifically,
\bar{T}_{\Theta}
can be written as

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t)=

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)+\Delta tM^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\Big(f(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta_{t,j})+\sum_{j=1}^{M}h(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{t,j})\Big)

(D.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)+\Delta tM^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j}).

The second proxy, denoted as
\widetilde{T}_{\rho}
, extends the width to infinity by letting
M\rightarrow\infty
, effectively replacing the average with an integral:

\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t)=\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)+\Delta t\int_{\beta}g(\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rho(\beta|t)d\beta.

(D.34)

We let all four Transformers share the same initial state
\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,0)=\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,0)=\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,0)=T_{\rho}(H,0)=H
.

We first present the following lemma, considering parameters i.i.d. drawn from some distribution
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
with bounded support:

Lemma D.6

(Oracle approximation of discretization)
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, suppose that the parameter setting
\Theta
is i.i.d. drawn from
\{\rho(\theta,w|t)\}_{t,j}
for some
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
concentrated on
\{(\theta,w):\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}+\lVert w\rVert^{2}\leq r^{2}\}\times[0,1]
and satisfies that
\int_{\theta,w}\rho(\theta,w,t)d(\theta,w)=1
for any
t\in[0,1]
. Then with probability at least
1-\exp(-\delta)
with respect to the parameter initialization
\Theta^{(0)}
, we have

\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

for any
H
,
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t,1
and any
\delta>0
. Here,
\lesssim
hides the dependencies on
N,D,r
and the parameters of the assumptions.

Proof of Proposition
3.3
.

Since
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2,r}
has a bounded support for any
\rho
, there exists some
\rho^{*}\in\pazocal{P}^{2,r}
such that
R(\rho^{*})=\inf_{\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2,r}}R(\rho)
. According to Lemma
D.6
, we can find a specific
\Theta
such that

\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{*}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq C\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big),

where
C
depends on
N,D,r
, and the parameters of the assumptions.
Moreover, from Lemma
D.6
, we ensure that each entry
\beta_{t,j}
of
\Theta
satisfies
\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert\leq r
. Verification of Lemmas
C.1
and
C.4
on
T_{\rho}
and
\widehat{T}\Theta
respectively leads to their uniform boundedness, i.e.,
\sup_{t}T_{\rho}(H,t)\lesssim 1
and
\sup_{t}\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\lesssim 1
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{R}(\Theta)-R(\rho^{*})|\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[|\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,1)-T_{\Theta}(H,1)]|\cdot||\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,1)+T_{\Theta}(H,1)]+2y(H)|]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(L+1)}{M}}.

Here,
\lesssim
hides the dependencies on
N,D,r
and the parameters of the assumptions. The result then follows.

The proof for the energy functional
Q
(and
\widehat{Q}
) follows a similar approach. There exists some
\rho^{*}\in\pazocal{P}^{2,r}
such that
Q(\rho^{*})=\inf_{\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2,r}}Q(\rho)
. From Lemmas
D.6
and
C.7
, we can find a specific
\Theta
such that

\begin{cases}&\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{*}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq C\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big),\\
&|\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert^{2}-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt|\leq C\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big).\end{cases}

by setting
C
large enough. Verification of Lemmas
C.1
and
C.4
on
T_{\rho}
and
\widehat{T}_{\Theta}
respectively leads to their uniform boundedness. Hence, we have

|\widehat{Q}(\Theta)-Q(\rho^{*})|\leq|\widehat{R}(\Theta)-R(\rho^{*})|+\lambda|\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert^{2}-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt|\leq C(1+\lambda)\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big).

The result thus follows.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs of main results in Section
4

For simplicity, we assume that Assumption
4
holds with
(g,\alpha)=(f,\theta)
. The proof for the case of
(g,\alpha)=(h,w)
is symmetric, involving a simple substitution of
f
with
h
and
\theta
with
w
.

E.1
Proofs of Theorem
4.1
and Corollary
4.1

Our proof of Theorem
4.1
consists of three parts, each focusing on bounding differences related to the energy functional
Q
or
\widehat{Q}
.

The first step establishes the continuity of the functional gradient
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\big|_{\rho_{\infty}}
. This ensures that if the derivative with respect to
\beta
for the functional gradient is constant over a region, then the functional gradient remains constant within that region.

The second step provides the key bound for
Q(\rho_{\infty})
, which is proportional to
\lambda
. This involves a detailed analysis of
Q
’s landscape by bounding its derivatives.

After obtaining the bound for
Q(\rho_{\infty})
, the final steps are to show that the finite-time risk can approach this bound. Achieving a loss as small as
\epsilon
requires
Q(\rho^{(\tau_{0})})\leq\epsilon
for some sufficiently large
\tau_{0}
. We then apply Theorem
3.1
, with constant dependency on
\tau_{0}
, to show that
\widehat{Q}(\tau_{0})
becomes sufficiently small. Since
\widehat{Q}(\rho^{(\tau)})
is non-increasing,
\widehat{Q}(\tau)
remains small for all
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
.

Preparatory Step: Landscape analysis

First, the following lemma suggests that as long as the risk
R(\rho)
remains positive, a descent direction for
Q(\rho)
can be constructed at any depth index, provided that
\lambda
is sufficiently small. This implies that by adjusting
\lambda
, one can influence the gradient flow to effectively reduce
Q(\rho)
.

Lemma E.1

(Landscape of
Q(\rho)
)
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
-
4
hold. For any
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
concentrated on
P_{r}
with some
r>0
, any
w_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}w}
, and any
t^{*}\in[0,1]
such that
\int_{\beta}\rho(\beta,t^{*})\geq 1/2
, there exists a
\nu\in\pazocal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}\beta})
such that

i.

for any
\beta\in\mathrm{supp}(\nu)
, we have
1/B_{r}\leq\lVert\beta\rVert\leq B_{r}

ii.

for any
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w)
, we have
\theta_{2}\in\pazocal{K}
and
w=w_{0}
.

iii.

\int_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}(\beta,t^{*})\Big(\nu(\beta)-\rho(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta\leq C_{1}\lambda-C_{2}R(\rho)

Here,
B_{r},C_{1},C_{2}
are constants that depends on
N,d,r
and the parameters of the assumptions.

Given the
t^{*}
and
w_{0}
specified in the theorem, Lemma
E.1
indicates that there exists some

\nu\in\pazocal{P}\Big(\Big(B_{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{1}}(0,B_{R_{\infty}})/B_{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{1}}(0,1/B_{R_{\infty}})\Big)\times\pazocal{K}\times\{w_{0}\}\Big)

such that
\int_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta,t^{*})\Big(\nu(\beta)-{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta\leq C_{1}\lambda-C_{2}R(\rho)
, where
B_{R_{\infty}},C_{1},C_{2}
are constants dependent on
N,d,R_{\infty}
and the parameters of the assumptions.

In addition, for any
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
, we define the following two functional derivatives:

\displaystyle\frac{\delta Q_{f}}{\delta\rho}(\theta,w,t)=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\Big[f(T_{\rho}(H,t),\theta)\Big]^{\top}p_{\rho}(H,t)\Big)\Big]+\lambda\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\frac{\delta Q_{h}}{\delta\rho}(\theta,w,t)=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\Big[h(T_{\rho}(H,t),w)\Big]^{\top}p_{\rho}(H,t)\Big)\Big]+\lambda\lVert w\rVert^{2}_{2}.

It is obvious that
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\equiv(\frac{\delta Q_{f}}{\delta\rho}+\frac{\delta Q_{h}}{\delta\rho})/2
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

Step I: Show that
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta,t)
is continuous with respect to
(\beta,t)

In Step I of the proof of Lemma
E.1
, we establish that
\rho_{\infty}\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
, with a bounded support
P_{R_{\infty}}
, implies
p_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t)
is
C_{p}
-Lipschitz continuous, where
C_{p}
is a constant dependent solely on
N,D,R_{\infty}
, and the parameters in our assumptions.

Next, we would like to show that
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}_{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta,t)
is continuous with respect to
(\beta,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{div}\beta}\times[0,1]
. Let’s focus on the region
(\beta,t)\in P_{r}
for any
r>R_{\infty}
, so that
\rho_{\infty}
is also concentrated on
P_{r}
.
It’s noteworthy that for any bounded support
(\beta,t)\in P_{r}
, Lemma
C.1
and Assumption
2
(i) ensure the universal boundedness of
g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)
, and Lemma
C.3
ensures the universal boundedness of
p_{\rho}(H,t)
for any
H\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, with the constants depending solely on
N,D,r
, and the parameters of the assumptions.

Combining the Lipschitz continuity of
p_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t)
and
T_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t)
with respect to
(H,t)
, as shown in Proposition
C.2
, along with the Lipschitz continuity of
g(T,\beta)
with respect to
(T,\beta)
when
\lVert T\rVert_{F}
is universally bounded (as guaranteed by Assumption
2
(ii) and (iii)), and their universal boundedness, we derive that
\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\Big[g(T_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t),\beta)\Big]^{\top}p_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t)\Big)
is
C_{G}
-Lipschitz continuous for
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{2}
with respect to
(\beta,t)\in P_{r}
for some constant
C_{G}
that depends only on
N,D,r
and the parameters of the assumptions. Since the Lipschitz constant
C_{G}
is independent of the choice of
H
, we see that
\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\Big[g(T_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t),\beta)\Big]^{\top}p_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t)\Big)
is uniformly continuous across all
H\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
with respect to
(\beta,t)\in P_{r}
. Consequently, we have that

\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}(\beta,t)|_{\rho_{\infty}}=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\mathrm{Tr}\Big(\Big[g(T_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t),\beta)\Big]^{\top}p_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t)\Big)\Big]+\frac{\lambda}{2}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}_{2}

is continuous with respect to
(\beta,t)\in P_{r}
. Since the choice of
r
is arbitrary, we conclude that
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta,t)
is continuous with respect to
(\beta,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{div}\beta}\times[0,1]
.

Step II: Show that
Q(\rho_{\infty})\lesssim\lambda
with further landscape analysis

In the first part of the proof, we will adopt a similar approach to Theorem 3.9 of
[
47
]
to demonstrate that the stationary point of the Wasserstein gradient flow, denoted
\rho_{\infty}
, satisfies
Q(\rho_{\infty})\lesssim\lambda
. It’s worth noting that in
[
47
]
, the authors assume
\lambda=0
and conclude
R(\rho_{\infty})=Q(\rho_{\infty})=0
, but this claim relies on assuming the global existence of the Wasserstein gradient flow rather than proving it directly.

Based on the pivotal findings from
[
53
]
regarding the stationary points in the Wasserstein space, we infer that the stationary point
\rho_{\infty}
of the Wasserstein gradient flow (
3.5
), i.e.

\frac{d\rho(\beta,t)}{d\tau}=\mathrm{div}_{\beta}\Big(\rho\nabla_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\Big),

must satisfy
\nabla_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}=0
almost everywhere over
\mathrm{supp}(\rho_{\infty})
. This further indicates that
\nabla_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w,t^{*})=0
almost everywhere over
\mathrm{supp}(\rho_{\infty}(\cdot,t^{*}))
.
The fact
\rho(\cdot,t^{*})
is a connected set, coupled with the continuity of the Frechét differential
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}
with respect to
\beta
, implies that,
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w,t^{*})=C
for some constant
C
over
(\beta,t^{*})\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho(\cdot,t^{*}))
.

Given the separation assumption on the support of
\rho_{\infty}(\cdot,t^{*})
, we ensure that for any
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\Big(B_{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{1}}(0,B_{R_{\infty}})/B_{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{1}}(0,1/B_{R_{\infty}})\Big)\times\pazocal{K}
, there exists
c\in\mathbb{R},\ 1/R_{\infty}B_{R_{\infty}}\leq|c|\leq R_{\infty}B_{R_{\infty}}
such that
(c\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho_{\infty})
. Combined with Assumption
4
(i), which implies the
1
-homogeneity of
f(T,\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
with respect to
\theta_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\delta Q_{f}}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(c\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})=c\frac{\delta Q_{f}}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})+(|c|-1)\lambda\lVert\theta_{1}\rVert^{2}.

(E.1)

\displaystyle\frac{\delta Q_{h}}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(c\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})=\frac{\delta Q_{h}}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*}).

Hence, given that
\nabla_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\cdot,t^{*})=0
almost everywhere over
\mathrm{supp}(\rho_{\infty}(\cdot,t^{*}))
, it also holds that

\displaystyle\nabla_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w)}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})=

\displaystyle\Big(\nabla_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w)}\frac{\delta Q_{f}}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})+\nabla_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w)}\frac{\delta Q_{h}}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})\Big)/2

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(-\frac{|c|-1}{c}\lambda\theta_{1},0_{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{2}},0_{w}\Big),

which implies

\left\lVert\nabla_{(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w)}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})\right\rVert\leq(R_{\infty}^{2}B_{R_{\infty}}+R_{\infty})\lambda.

(E.2)

Given the condition that
\lVert(c\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})-(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})\rVert\leq R_{\infty}^{2}B_{R_{\infty}}
, and recalling that
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w,t^{*})\equiv C
across
(\beta,t^{*})\in\mathrm{supp}(\rho(\cdot,t^{*}))
, (
E.2
) further indicates that

\Big|\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2},w_{0},t^{*})-C\Big|\leq(R_{\infty}^{4}B_{R_{\infty}}^{2}+R_{\infty}^{3}B_{R_{\infty}})\lambda.

(E.3)

for any
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\Big(B_{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{1}}(0,B_{R_{\infty}})/B_{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{1}}(0,1/B_{R_{\infty}})\Big)\times\pazocal{K}
.
Hence, by Lemma
E.1
we have

\displaystyle C_{1}\lambda-C_{2}R(\rho_{\infty})\geq

\displaystyle\int_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta,t^{*})\Big(\nu(\beta)-{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta

(E.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\beta}\Big(\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta,t^{*})-C\Big)\Big(\nu(\beta)-{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\int_{\beta}(R_{\infty}^{4}B_{R_{\infty}}^{2}+R_{\infty}^{3}B_{R_{\infty}})\lambda\Big(\nu(\beta)+{\rho_{\infty}}(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2(R_{\infty}^{4}B_{R_{\infty}}^{2}+R_{\infty}^{3}B_{R_{\infty}})\lambda.

Therefore, we have
R(\rho_{\infty})\leq\frac{C_{1}+2(R_{\infty}^{4}B_{R_{\infty}}^{2}+R_{\infty}^{3}B_{R_{\infty}})}{C_{2}}\lambda
, and

Q(\rho_{\infty})\leq R(\rho_{\infty})+\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt\leq\Big(\frac{C_{1}+2(R_{\infty}^{4}B_{R_{\infty}}^{2}+R_{\infty}^{3}B_{R_{\infty}})}{C_{2}}+R_{\infty}^{2}\Big)\lambda,

(E.5)

which completes the first part of our proof.

Step III: Bound the difference between
Q(\rho^{(\tau)})
and
Q(\rho_{\infty})
when
\tau
is large

Proposition
3.2
establishes that the second moment for
\rho^{(\tau)}
is uniformly bounded across all
\tau\geq 0
:

\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho^{(\tau)}(\beta,t)d\beta dt\leq A_{0}^{2},

where
A_{0}
is defined as in Proposition
3.2
. Therefore, the weak convergence of probability measures
(\rho^{(\tau)})_{\tau\geq 0}
is equivalent to the convergence in the Wasserstein-
2
distance, i.e.

\lim_{\tau\rightarrow\infty}W_{2}(\rho^{(\tau)},\rho_{\infty})=0.

(E.6)

When
\tau
is sufficiently large,
\rho^{(\tau)}
concentrates on
P_{R_{\infty}}
. Therefore, according to Lemma
C.2
, there exists a constant
C_{0}
depending solely on
N
,
D
,
R_{\infty}
, and the parameters of the assumptions that

\lVert T_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t)-T_{\rho^{(\tau)}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq C_{0}W_{2}(\rho^{(\tau)},\rho_{\infty})

(E.7)

for any
H
and
t\in[0,1]
when
\tau
is sufficiently large. Note that Lemma
C.1
shows that

\max\{\lVert T_{\rho^{(\tau)}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},\lVert T_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\}\leq B\exp(K(1+R_{\infty}+R_{\infty}^{2}))=:B_{T}

for any
H
and
t\in[0,1]
. Thus, from (
E.7
), we have

\displaystyle|Q(\rho_{\infty})-Q(\rho^{(\tau)})|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[|\mathrm{Read}[|T_{\rho^{(\tau)}}(H,1)]|+|T_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,1)]|+2|y(H)|\Big]\lVert T_{\rho_{\infty}}(H,1)-T_{\rho^{(\tau)}}(H,1)\rVert_{F}

(E.8)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}(\rho_{\infty}-\rho^{(\tau)})(\beta,t)d\beta dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(B_{T}+B)C_{0}W_{2}(\rho^{(\tau)},\rho_{\infty})+\frac{\lambda}{2}R_{\infty}W_{1}(\rho^{(\tau)},\rho_{\infty})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle((B_{T}+B)C_{0}+\frac{\lambda}{2}R_{\infty})W_{2}(\rho^{(\tau)},\rho_{\infty}),

where the second inequality incorporates the Kantorovich-Rubinstein Theorem (see Theorem 5.10 of
[
68
]
, for example) and the
2R_{\infty}
-Lipschitz continuity of
\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}
over the region
(\beta,t)\in P_{R_{\infty}}
.
Combining equations (
E.6
) and (
E.8
), we deduce that for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists some
\tau_{0}>0
such that
|Q(\rho_{\infty})-Q(\rho^{(\tau_{0})})|\leq\epsilon
.

Step IV: Complete the proof by bounding the difference between
\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau)})
and
Q(\rho^{(\tau)})
when
\tau
is large

The final step can be seen as a direct corollary of the approximation result in Theorem
3.1
.
According to Theorem
3.1
, there exists a constant
C_{1}
dependent on
N
,
D
,
\tau_{0}
,
\lambda
, and the parameters specified in the assumptions, such that

|\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau_{0})})-Q(\rho^{(\tau_{0}))})|\leq C_{1}\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big)

with probability at least
1-3\exp(-\delta)
with respect to the parameter initialization
\Theta^{(0)}
for any
\delta>0
. Combining the outcomes from the preceding steps, we obtain

\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau_{0})})\leq\epsilon+C_{1}\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big)+C_{2}\lambda.

(E.9)

Note that

\frac{d}{d\tau}\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau)})=\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\langle\frac{d\widehat{Q}(\Theta)}{d\beta_{t,j}}\bigg|_{\Theta=\Theta^{(\tau)}},-ML\frac{d\widehat{Q}(\Theta)}{d\beta_{t,j}}\bigg|_{\Theta=\Theta^{(\tau)}}\rangle=-\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\widehat{G}_{\beta}(\beta^{(\tau)}_{t,j},\Theta^{(\tau)},t)\rVert^{2}\leq 0,

so the sequence
(\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau)}))_{\tau\geq 0}
is non-decreasing. Hence, for any
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
,

\widehat{R}(\Theta^{(\tau)})\leq\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau)})\leq\widehat{Q}(\Theta^{(\tau_{0})})\leq\epsilon+C_{1}\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big)+C_{2}\lambda,

which completes the proof, recalling that
C_{1}
depends only on
N,D,\tau_{0},\lambda
and the parameters of the assumptions, and
C_{2}
depends only on
N,D,R_{\infty}
, and the parameters of the assumptions.

∎

Proof of Corollary
4.1
.

Given the choice of
\lambda
By Theorem
4.1
, there exists some
\tau_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\geq\tau_{0}}\widehat{R}(\Theta^{(\tau)})\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon/2+C_{1}\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big)+C_{2}C_{\lambda}\lambda

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1/4+C_{2}C_{\lambda})\epsilon+C_{1}\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big).

The result holds by setting
L
and
M/\log L
sufficiently large to ensure that

C_{1}\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big)\leq C_{1}\Big(L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{2(1+\delta)\log(L+1)}{M}}\Big)\leq\epsilon/4.

∎

Appendix F
Proofs of auxiliary results

F.1
Proof of Lemma
D.1

Proof.

Lemma
C.1
confirms that
\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
and
\lVert T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
are bounded uniformly by
B_{T}=B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))
. Considering the definition (
C.2
), it suffices to demonstrate that

\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\beta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)^{\top}p_{\rho}(H,t)\Big)-\nabla_{\beta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\widetilde{\beta})^{\top}p_{\nu}(H,t)\Big)\Big]\right\rVert\leq J_{1}+J_{2},

where

\displaystyle J_{1}:=\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\beta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big((g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\widetilde{\beta})-g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta))^{\top}p_{\nu}(H,t)\Big)\Big]\right\rVert\lesssim\lVert\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\rVert,

\displaystyle J_{2}:=\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\beta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)^{\top}(p_{\nu}(H,t)-p_{\rho}(H,t))\Big)\Big]\right\rVert\lesssim\exp(C_{G}W_{1}(\rho,\nu))-1.

Here, the symbol
\lesssim
hides dependencies on
N
,
D
,
r
, and the parameters of the assumptions.
To bound
J_{1}
, consider that

\displaystyle J_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\left\lVert g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\widetilde{\beta})_{:,i}-g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\left\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)_{:,i}\right\rVert\Big]

(F.1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\left\lVert g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\widetilde{\beta})_{:,i}-g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\left\lVert g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\widetilde{\beta})_{:,i}-g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\phi_{PT}(r,B_{T})\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}+\phi_{P}P(r,B_{T})\lVert\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle W_{1}(\rho,\nu)+\lVert\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\rVert.

The third inequality in Equation (
F.1
) is derived from Lemma
C.3
, while the fourth inequality relies on Assumption
3
(i) and (iii). Lastly, bounding
\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
by
W_{1}(\rho,\nu)
is achieved with Lemma
C.2
.

On the other hand, to bound
J_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\left\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)-p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\Big]

(F.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\lVert(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)-p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)-p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}.

In (
F.2
), the third inequality relies on Assumption
2
(i). Consequently, to establish
J_{2}\lesssim\exp(C_{G}W_{1}(\rho,\nu))-1
, it is adequate to demonstrate that
\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)-p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq I_{1}+I_{2}\lesssim W_{1}(\rho,\nu)
, where

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\nu}(H,1)]|\left\lVert\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)\right\rVert,

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)|

\displaystyle\left\lVert\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)-\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)\right\rVert.

From Lemma
C.2
, it is trivial that
|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\nu}(H,1)]|\leq\lVert T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\nu}(H,1)\rVert_{F}\lesssim W_{1}(\rho,\nu)
. Thus,
I_{1}\lesssim W_{1}(\rho,\nu)
given the boundedness of
\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)]\rVert
as provided in Assumption
2
(iii). To bound
I_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\lVert\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)-\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)\right\rVert

(F.3)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left\lVert\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds-\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)-I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]-\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)]\right\rVert\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)-1

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\phi_{TT}(N,D,B_{T},r)\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\Big)-1

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\exp\Big(C_{r}\phi_{TT}(N,D,B_{T},r)\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+r+r^{2})W_{1}(\rho,\nu)\Big)-1

for some constant
C_{r}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result setting.
Here, the first inequality in (
F.3
) stems from
|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)|\leq B_{T}+B
, the second inequality is ensured by the boundedness of
\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)]\rVert
as stated in Assumption
2
(iii), and the fourth inequality is provided by Assumption
3
(iv). The last inequality in (
F.3
) arises from Assumption
2
(iii) and Lemma
C.2
. By combining Equation (
F.3
) with the bounds of
J_{1}
and
I_{1}
, we deduce that
J_{1}+J_{2}\lesssim\exp(C_{G}W_{1}(\rho,\nu))-1+\lVert\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\rVert
for some constant
C_{G}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result, thereby completing the proof.
∎

F.2
Proof of Lemma
D.2

Proof.

Lemma
C.4
demonstrates that
\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
and
\lVert\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
are bounded by
B_{T}=B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))
for any
H
and
t\in[0,1]
.
We begin by bounding

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta,t)-\widehat{G}(\beta,\widetilde{\Theta},t)\rVert=

\displaystyle\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta)-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)\Big)^{\top}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\Big]^{\top}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{Tr}f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)^{\top}\Big(\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{p}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\Big)\Big]^{\top},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{w}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2),w)-h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2),w)\Big)^{\top}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t)\Big]^{\top}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{w}\mathrm{Tr}h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2),w)^{\top}\Big(\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widehat{p}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\Big)\Big]^{\top}\Big\}^{\top}.

To demonstrate that
\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta,t)-\widehat{G}(\beta,\widetilde{\Theta},t)\rVert\leq C_{G}\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})
, it suffices to show

J_{1}:=\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta)-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)\Big)^{\top}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\Big]\right\rVert\leq C_{G}\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta}),

(F.4)

and

J_{2}:=\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{Tr}f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)^{\top}\Big(\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{p}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\Big)\Big]\right\rVert\leq C_{G}\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta}),

(F.5)

as the other part for
h
and
w
follows a similar proof approach.

To bound
J_{1}
, by Assumption
3
(i) we have

\displaystyle J_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\left\lVert\nabla_{\theta}f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta)_{:,i}-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\left\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)_{:,i}\right\rVert\Big]

(F.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\left\lVert\nabla_{\theta}f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta)_{:,i}-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\left\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)_{:,i}\right\rVert\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\phi_{T}(r,B_{T})\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\sqrt{N+1}\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\phi_{T}(r,B_{T})\sqrt{N+1}\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta}),

where the fourth inequality utilizes Lemma
C.5
and the last inequality uses Lemma
C.6
.

To bound
J_{2}
, by Assumption
2
(ii), we have

\displaystyle J_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\left\lVert f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)_{:,i}\right\rVert\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{p}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}\Big]

(F.7)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\phi(B_{T})(1+r)\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{p}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}\Big].

Hence, it suffices to show that
\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{p}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}\lesssim\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})
to establish
J_{2}\leq\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})
. Recalling the formula
\widehat{p}_{\Theta}
in (
C.16
), we have
\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{p}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}\leq I_{1}+I_{2}
, where

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,1)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,1)]

\displaystyle\Big\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\Big)

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]\Big)\Big\}_{DN+d+1,:}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})

\displaystyle\Big\lVert\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\Big)

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]\Big)\Big\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})

\displaystyle\exp\Big((\Delta t/2)\sum_{(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\right\rVert

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Delta t/2)\sum_{(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{s,j})]\right\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB_{T})(1+r+r^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta}),

and

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,1)]+y(H)|

\displaystyle\Big\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\Big)

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]\Big)-

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{\theta}_{s,j})]\Big)

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta/2),\widetilde{w}_{s,j})]\Big)\Big\}_{DN+d+1,:}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(B+B_{T})

\displaystyle\cdot\exp\Big((\Delta t/2)\sum_{(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\max\{\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\right\rVert,\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{\theta}_{s,j})]\}\right\rVert

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Delta t/2)\sum_{(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\max\{\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{s,j})]\right\rVert,\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{w}_{s,j})]\}\right\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot(\Delta t/2)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(\left\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]-M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{\theta}_{s,j})]\right\rVert

\displaystyle+\left\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{s,j})]-M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{w}_{s,j})]\right\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(B+B_{T})\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB_{T})(1+r+r^{2})\Big)\phi_{TP}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T},r)\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta}).

where the first inequality applies Lemma
C.8
, and the second inequality relies on Assumption
2
(iii) and Assumption
3
(ii). Therefore, we conclude that
I_{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})
. By combining the bounds of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we observe that Equation (
F.5
) holds, thereby establishing
\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta,t)-\widehat{G}(\beta,\widetilde{\Theta},t)\rVert\leq C_{G}\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})
for some
C_{G}
dependent on
N
,
d
,
r
, and the parameters of the assumptions.

It remains to prove that
\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\widetilde{\Theta},t)-\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\Theta},t)\rVert\leq C_{G}(1+\lambda)\lVert\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\rVert
. Note that

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\widetilde{\Theta},t)-\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\Theta},t)\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\lambda\lVert\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\rVert

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big\{\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{\theta})\Big)^{\top}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\Big]^{\top},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{w}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2),w)-h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2),\widetilde{w})\Big)^{\top}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t)\Big]^{\top}\Big\}^{\top}.

Therefore, we only need to show that

\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{\theta})\Big)^{\top}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\Big]\right\rVert\leq C_{G}\lVert\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\rVert,

and

\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{w}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),w)-h(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{w})\Big)^{\top}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t)\Big]\right\rVert\leq C_{G}\lVert w-\widetilde{w}\rVert,

to obtain
\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\widetilde{\Theta},t)-\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\Theta},t)\rVert\leq C_{G}(1+\lambda)\lVert\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\rVert
. Here, we only establish the inequality above for
f
and
\theta
, as the proof of the other inequality follows a similar pattern. Note that by Assumption
3
(iii), we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big(f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{\theta})\Big)^{\top}\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\Big]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in[N+1]}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\Big[\left\lVert\nabla_{\theta}\Big(f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\theta)-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{\theta})\Big)_{:,i}\right\rVert\Big]\sqrt{N+1}\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\phi_{PP}(r,B_{T})\lVert\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\rVert\sqrt{N+1}\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\theta-\widetilde{\theta}\rVert.

where the last inequality applies Lemma
C.6
. Therefore, we conclude that
\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\widetilde{\Theta},t)-\widehat{G}(\widetilde{\beta},\widetilde{\Theta},t)\rVert\leq C_{G}(1+\lambda)\lVert\beta-\widetilde{\beta}\rVert
, completing the proof.
∎

F.3
Proof of Lemma
D.3

Proof.

Lemmas
C.1
and
C.4
establish that
\max{\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}}\leq B_{T}:=B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))
. According to Lemma
D.6
, there exists an event
E
with
\mathbb{P}(E)\geq 1-\exp(-\delta)
such that under
E
, we have

\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

(F.8)

for any
H
and
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t,1
. Following the same proof procedure as in Lemma
D.6
, with
\rho
replaced by
\widehat{\rho}
, and bounding only
J_{1}
and
J_{3}
in the proof (as there is no need to utilize Hoeffding’s inequality to bridge the difference due to a finite width
M
), we could obtain the bound

\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-T_{\widehat{\rho}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim L^{-1}.

(F.9)

We present the proof only for the case involving
\rho
. The bounding of
\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta,t)-G(\beta,\widehat{\rho},t)\rVert_{F}
can be derived analogously by substituting
\rho
with
\widehat{\rho}
using Equation (
F.9
), and skipping the process of bounding
\lVert D_{1}-D_{2}\rVert
where
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
will be defined later. The bounding of
\left\lVert G(\beta,\widehat{\rho},t)-G(\beta,\rho,t)\right\rVert
can be straightforwardly achieved by combining the results obtained from the other two cases.

By the definitions of the gradients in Equations (
C.2
) and (
C.3
), we observe that
\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta,t)-G(\beta,\rho,t)\right\rVert\lesssim\left\lVert\widehat{G}_{f}(\theta,\Theta,t)-G_{f}(\theta,\rho,t)\right\rVert+\left\lVert\widehat{G}_{h}(w,\Theta,t)-G_{h}(w,\rho,t)\right\rVert
. We will focus on showing that
\left\lVert 2(\widehat{G}_{f}(\theta,\Theta,t)-G_{f}(\theta,\rho,t))\right\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
, as the other part of the proof follows a similar approach.

Let’s define the following quantities
A_{1},A_{2},B_{1},B_{2},C_{1},C_{2}
:

\displaystyle A_{1}:=

\displaystyle\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta)],\quad A_{2}:=\nabla_{\theta}\mathrm{vec}[f(T_{\rho}(H,t),\theta)]

\displaystyle B_{1}:=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,1)-y(H)],\quad B_{2}:=\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)-y(H)]

\displaystyle C_{1}:=

\displaystyle\Big\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\Big)

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]\Big)\Big\}_{DN+d+1,:}

\displaystyle C_{2}:=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt\Big)_{DN+d+1,:}

The universal boundedness of
\lVert A_{1}\rVert
and
\lVert A_{2}\rVert
is implied by Assumption
2
(ii), while the universal boundedness of
|B_{1}|
and
|B_{2}|
is implied by Assumption
1
. Additionally,
\lVert C_{1}\rVert
and
\lVert C_{2}\rVert
can be bounded via Assumption
2
(iii), and one can refer to the proofs of Lemmas
C.3
and
C.6
for detailed explanations.

From (
C.14
) and (
C.16
), we could rewrite
J:=\left\lVert 2(\widehat{G}_{f}(\theta,\Theta,t)-G_{f}(\theta,\rho,t))\right\rVert
as

\displaystyle J=\lVert A_{1}B_{1}C_{1}-A_{2}B_{2}C_{2}\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\lVert(A_{1}-A_{2})B_{2}C_{2}\rVert+\lVert A_{1}(B_{1}-B_{2})C_{2}\rVert+\lVert A_{1}B_{1}(C_{1}-C_{2})\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert A_{1}-A_{2}\rVert\lVert B_{2}\rVert\lVert C_{2}\rVert+\lVert A_{1}\rVert\lVert B_{1}-B_{2}\rVert\lVert C_{2}\rVert+\lVert A_{1}\rVert\lVert B_{1}\rVert\lVert C_{1}-C_{2}\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert A_{1}-A_{2}\rVert+\lVert B_{1}-B_{2}\rVert+\lVert C_{1}-C_{2}\rVert.

We claim that to obtain the result, it suffices to show that

i.

\lVert A_{1}-A_{2}\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
under event
E
.

ii.

\lVert B_{1}-B_{2}\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
under event
E
.

iii.

There exists some event
E_{2}
with
\mathbb{P}(E_{2})\geq 1-\exp(-\delta)
such that
\lVert C_{1}-C_{2}\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
under
E\cap E_{2}
.

This is because if we can establish the above statements, then under the event
E\cap E_{2}
with
\mathbb{P}(E\cap E_{2})\geq 1-2\exp(-\delta)
, we obtain
J=\left\lVert 2(\widehat{G}_{f}(\theta,\Theta,t)-G_{f}(\theta,\rho,t))\right\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
. Given the similarity in proof for
\left\lVert 2(\widehat{G}_{h}(w,\Theta,t)-G_{h}(w,\rho,t))\right\rVert
, we deduce that with probability at least
1-4\exp(-\delta)
with respect to the parameter initialization
\Theta^{(0)}
, we have
\left\lVert\widehat{G}(\beta,\Theta,t)-G(\beta,\rho,t)\right\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.
The remainder of the proof focuses on bounding the quantities in statements (i)-(iii).

Proof for statement (i):
By Assumption
3
(iv), we have
\lVert A_{1}-A_{2}\rVert\leq\phi_{TT}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T},r)\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
under event
E
.

Proof for statement (ii):
Under event
E
, it is obvious to see
\lVert B_{1}-B_{2}\rVert\leq\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
.

Proof for statement (iii):
We further define the following quantities

\displaystyle D_{1}:=

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\Big)

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]\Big)

\displaystyle D_{2}:=

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta)]\rho(\beta|s)d\beta\Big)

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}\Big(I_{\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{vec}[T]}+(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w)]\rho(\beta|s)d\beta\Big)

\displaystyle D_{3}:=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta\Big)

\displaystyle D_{4}:=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt\Big)

Note that
\lVert C_{1}-C_{2}\rVert\leq\lVert D_{1}-D_{2}\rVert+\lVert D_{2}-D_{3}\rVert+\lVert D_{3}-D_{4}\rVert
.
Assumption
2
(iii) indicates that for any
s=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta
and
j=1,\dots,M
, we have

\max\{\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\rVert,\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]\rVert\}\leq B_{J}

for some constant
B_{J}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result. This implies that each column of
\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]
or
\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]
has
l_{2}
norm upper bounded by
B_{J}
as well.
Applying Hoeffding’s inequality to each column of
\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]
and
\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]
