, and subsequently calculating the union bound across all columns yields:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\left\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]-\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\rho(\beta|s)d\beta\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{(N+1)D}z\Big)

(F.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(N+1)D\exp(-\frac{z^{2}}{2B_{J}^{2}}M)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\left\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{s,j})]-\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{s,j})]\rho(\beta|s)d\beta\right\rVert\geq\sqrt{(N+1)D}z\Big)

(F.11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(N+1)D\exp(-\frac{z^{2}}{2B_{J}^{2}}M)

for any
z>0
. For (
F.10
) and (
F.11
), we further the consider the union bound across all
s=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta
, and let
z=B_{J}\sqrt{2M(\delta+\log(2(N+1)DL))}
, which implies that with probability at least
1-\exp(-\delta)
with respect to the parameter initialization
\Theta^{(0)}
, we have

\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]-\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\rho(\beta|s)d\beta\rVert

and

\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{s,j})]-\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{s,j})]\rho(\beta|s)d\beta\rVert

bounded by
B_{J}\sqrt{2M(N+1)D(\delta+\log(2(N+1)DL))}\leq C_{J}\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
for any
s=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta
. Here,
C_{J}
is some constant that only depends on
N,D,r
and the parameters of the assumptions. Denote this probability event by
E_{2}
, and we have
\mathbb{P}(E_{2})\geq 1-\exp(-\delta)
. Under
E_{2}
, by Lemma
C.8
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert D_{1}-D_{2}\rVert

(F.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2L}\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}\left\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]-\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta\right\rVert

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}\left\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),w_{s,j})]-\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta\right\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{J}\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

For any
s=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta
, we define

A_{s,j}=(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta)]\rho(\beta|s)d\beta

and

B_{s,j}=(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w)]\rho(\beta|s)d\beta.

Since Assumption
2
(iii) indicates that
\max\{\lVert A_{s,j}\rVert,\lVert B_{s,j}\rVert\}\lesssim\Delta t=L^{-1}
, we have

\left\lVert\exp(A_{s,j})-I-A_{s,j}\right\rVert\lesssim\lVert A_{s,j}\rVert^{2},\quad\left\lVert\exp(B_{s,j})-I-B_{s,j}\right\rVert\lesssim\lVert B_{s,j}\rVert^{2}.

Applying Lemma
C.8
once more, we have

\lVert D_{2}-D_{3}\rVert\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}\left\lVert A_{s,j}\right\rVert^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}\left\lVert B_{s,j}\right\rVert^{2}\lesssim L^{-1}.

(F.13)

Since Assumption
2
(iii) ensures the boundedness of
\lVert D_{4}\rVert
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert D_{3}-D_{4}\rVert\lesssim

\displaystyle\Big\lVert\exp\Big(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta

(F.14)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta

\displaystyle-\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt\Big)-1\Big\rVert.

Therefore, to show that
\lVert D_{3}-D_{4}\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}},
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle J_{34}:=

\displaystyle\Big\lVert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+2\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta

\displaystyle-\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt\Big\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}.

By Assumption
3
(iv) and Lemma
D.6
, we have

\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta)]-\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(T_{\rho}(H,s),\theta)]\right\rVert\lesssim\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s)-T_{\rho}(H,s)\rVert_{F}\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}},

and

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w)]-\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(T_{\rho}(H,s),w)]\right\rVert\lesssim\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2)-T_{\rho}(H,s)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s)\rVert_{F}+\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s)-T_{\rho}(H,s)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}+L^{-1}\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),w_{s,j})\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}},

where the last inequality employs Assumption
2
(i). Therefore, we conclude that

\displaystyle\lVert D_{3}-D_{4}\rVert\lesssim J_{34}\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}+\lVert(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(T_{\rho}(H,t),\theta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta\rVert

(F.15)

\displaystyle\Big\lVert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(T_{\rho}(H,s),\theta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\end{subarray}}(\Delta t/2)\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(T_{\rho}(H,s)(H,s),w)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta

\displaystyle-\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt\Big\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}+\sup_{|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq\Delta t}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s_{1}),\beta)]-\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s_{2}),\beta)]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}+\sup_{|s_{1}-s_{2}|\leq\Delta t}\lVert T_{\rho}(H,s_{1})-T_{\rho}(H,s_{2})\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}

where the third inequality uses Assumption
3
(iv), and the last inequality relies on the Lipschitz continuity as demonstrated in Proposition
C.1
. Combining (
F.12
), (
F.13
), and (
F.15
) yields
\lVert C_{1}-C_{2}\rVert\leq\lVert D_{1}-D_{2}\rVert+\lVert D_{2}-D_{3}\rVert+\lVert D_{3}-D_{4}\rVert\lesssim L^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{\delta+\log(L+1)}{M}}
.
∎

F.4
Proof of Lemma
D.4

Proof.

Define
F_{\rho}(T_{\bar{\rho}},t)=\int_{\beta}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\bar{\rho}}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta
for any
\rho,\bar{\rho}\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
.
From Taylor’s expansion, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{vec}[\dot{T}_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-\dot{T}_{\rho}(H,t)]

\displaystyle=F_{\rho}(T_{\rho_{\eta}},t)-F_{\rho}(T_{\rho},t)+F_{\rho_{\eta}}(T_{\rho_{\eta}},t)-F_{\rho}(T_{\rho_{\eta}},t)

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta\Big)\Big(\mathrm{vec}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)]\Big)

\displaystyle+\eta\int_{\beta}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t),\beta)](\nu-\rho)(\beta,t)d\beta+o(\eta)

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta\Big)\Big(\mathrm{vec}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)]\Big)

\displaystyle+\eta\int_{\beta}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)](\nu-\rho)(\beta,t)d\beta

\displaystyle+\eta\Big(\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)](\nu-\rho)(\beta,t)d\beta\Big)\Big(\mathrm{vec}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)]\Big)+o(\eta)

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta\Big)\Big(\mathrm{vec}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)]\Big)

\displaystyle+\eta\int_{\beta}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)](\nu-\rho)(\beta,t)d\beta+o(\eta),

of which the last equality holds as Lemma
C.2
shows that
\lVert T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}=O(W_{2}(\rho_{\eta},\rho))=O(\eta)
, where we hide the constant dependence on
B,K,N,r
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\Big\{\exp\Big(-\int_{0}^{\top}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta)\Big)\Big(\mathrm{vec}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)]\Big)\Big\}

(F.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\int_{0}^{\top}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta)\Big)

\displaystyle\Big\{\mathrm{vec}[\dot{T}_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-\dot{T}_{\rho}(H,t)]-\Big(\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta\Big)\Big(\mathrm{vec}[T_{\rho_{\eta}}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)]\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(-\int_{0}^{\top}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta)\Big)\Big\{\eta\int_{\beta}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)](\nu-\rho)(\beta,t)d\beta+o(\eta)\Big\},

which leads to (
D.18
).
∎

F.5
Proof of Lemma
D.5

Proof.

Fix
(\beta,t)\in P_{r}
.
Lemma
C.2
implies that

\lVert p_{\rho}(H,1)-p_{\nu}(H,1)\rVert_{F}=|\mathrm{Read}(T_{\rho}(H,1)-\mathrm{Read}(T_{\nu}(H,1)|\leq C_{r}W_{1}(\rho,\nu)\leq C_{r}(r+1)\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}

for some constant
C_{r}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result. Our goal is to regulate the difference between
\dot{p}_{\rho}(H,t)
and
\dot{p}_{\nu}(H,t)
to control the the propagation of
\lVert p_{\rho}(H,\cdot)-p_{\nu}(H,\cdot)\rVert
.
Note that by (
C.14
) and Assumption
2
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)-p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\dot{p}_{\rho}(H,t)-\dot{p}_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

(F.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big\lVert\mathrm{vec}[p_{\rho}(H,t)]^{\top}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta

\displaystyle-\mathrm{vec}[p_{\nu}(H,t)]^{\top}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)]\nu(\beta,t)d\beta\Big\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)-p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\right\rVert\rho(\beta,t)d\beta

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)]\right\rVert(\rho-\nu)(\beta,t)d\beta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{r,1}\Big(\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)-p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\int_{\beta}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta+\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\lVert\rho(\cdot,t)-\nu(\cdot,t)\rVert_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{r,1}\Big(\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)-p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\int_{\beta}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta+L_{r,2}\lVert\rho(\cdot,t)-\nu(\cdot,t)\rVert_{1}\Big)

where

L_{r,1}=\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2})))(1+r+r^{2})

and

L_{r,2}=(B+B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2})))\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))(1+r+r^{2})\Big).

The third inequality of (
F.17
) uses Lemma
C.1
to obtain

\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{N+1}\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq\sqrt{N+1}B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))

and

\lVert T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{N+1}\lVert T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq\sqrt{N+1}B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))

with Assumption
2
(iii) to bound the norm of the Jacobian matrix with
L_{r,1}
. The last inequality of (
F.17
) employs Lemma
C.3
to bound
\lVert p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}
with
L_{r,2}
.
Applying the Grönwall’s inequality to (
F.17
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)-p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq

\displaystyle C_{r}(r+1)\exp(L_{r,1})\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}+\int_{0}^{1}L_{r,1}L_{r,2}\exp(L_{r,1}\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta ds)\lVert\rho(\cdot,t)-\nu(\cdot,t)\rVert_{1}dt

(F.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{r}(r+1)\exp(L_{r,1})\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}+L_{r,1}L_{r,2}\exp(L_{r,1})\int_{0}^{1}\lVert\rho(\cdot,t)-\nu(\cdot,t)\rVert_{1}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(C_{r}+L_{r,1}L_{r,2})\exp(L_{r,1}))\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}

Since

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\lVert\rho(\cdot,t)-\nu(\cdot,t)\rVert_{1}dt=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}|\rho(\beta,t)-\nu(\beta,t)|d\beta dt=\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}.

Thus, we complete the proof of the first result.

By Lemma
C.3
, under Assumption
1
we have

\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq L_{r,3}:=(B+B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2})))\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))(1+r+r^{2})\Big).

In addition, by Lemma
C.1
, under Assumption
2
(i) we have

\lVert g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{N+1}\lVert g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq L_{r,4}:=KB\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))(1+r+r^{2}).

Therefore, for the gradient function
\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}
, by Lemma
C.3
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\rho}(\beta,t)-\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}\bigg|_{\nu}(\beta,t)\Big|=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\mathrm{Tr}(g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)^{\top}p_{\rho}(H,t))-\mathrm{Tr}(g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)^{\top}p_{\nu}(H,t))]

(F.19)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\lVert g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)-p_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}]

\displaystyle\leq L_{r,3}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}]+L_{r,4}(C_{r}+L_{r,1}L_{r,2})\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{N+1}L_{r,3}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}]+L_{r,4}(C_{r}+L_{r,1}L_{r,2})\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}.

Hence, it suffices to show that for any
H
such that
\lVert H\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B
, we have
\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq L_{r,5}\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1}
for some
L_{r,5}>0
in order to obtain the second result of this lemma. By Assumption
2
(iii), we see that

\displaystyle\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq

\displaystyle\phi_{T}(N,D,\max\{\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F},\lVert T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\})(1+r+r^{2})\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2}

(F.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+r+r^{2})))(1+r+r^{2})C_{r}W_{1}(\rho,\nu)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+r+r^{2})))(1+r+r^{2})C_{r}(1+r)\lVert\rho-\nu\rVert_{1},

where the second inequality again uses Lemma (
C.2
). Combining (
F.19
) and
F.20
completes the proof of the second result.
∎

F.6
Proof of Lemma
D.6

Proof.

Denote the empirical distribution of
\bar{T}_{\Theta}
and
\widetilde{T}_{\rho}
by

\bar{\rho}=\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\delta(\beta_{t,j},t),\quad\widetilde{\rho}=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{t}\delta_{t}(t)\rho(\beta|t),

respectively. It’s straightforward to verify that
\bar{\rho}
and
\widetilde{\rho}
meet the conditions outlined in Lemma
C.1
, and
T_{\bar{\rho}}=\bar{T}_{\Theta}
and
T_{\widetilde{\rho}}=\widetilde{T}_{\rho}
. Hence, Lemmas
C.1
and
C.4
indicate that
\max\{\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},\lVert\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},\lVert\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\}\leq B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))
for any
H
and
t\in[0,1]
. We then define
B_{T}:=B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))
.

The following decomposition equation holds:

\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq\underbrace{\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{F}}_{J_{1}}+\underbrace{\lVert\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}}_{J_{2}}+\underbrace{\lVert\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}}_{J_{3}},

(F.21)

Our proof will bound
J_{1},J_{2}
and
J_{3}
, possibly in a probabilistic manner, to obtain the desired result.

Bounding
J_{1}
:

Note that according to Assumption
2
(i),

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta_{t,j})\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta_{t,j})\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}+(K\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}(1+\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle B_{T}(1+(K\Delta t/2)(1+r+r^{2}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle B_{T}(1+(K/2)(1+r+r^{2}))

Denote
B_{T}(1+(K/2)(1+r+r^{2}))
by
\bar{B}_{T}
.
Combining the two equations in (
2.4
) gives us

\displaystyle\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t)=\mathrm{MLP}_{w_{t,1},\dots,w_{t,M}}\Big(\mathrm{Attn}_{\theta_{t,1},\dots,\theta_{t,M}}(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\Delta t/2),\Delta t/2\Big)

(F.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Attn}_{\theta_{t,1},\dots,\theta_{t,M}}(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\Delta t/2)+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}h\Big(\mathrm{Attn}_{\theta_{t,1},\dots,\theta_{t,M}}(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\Delta t/2),w_{t,j}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta_{t,j})+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}h\Big(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta_{t,j}),w_{t,j}\Big)

Then, from the formula of (
F.22
) and Assumption
2
(iii), we see that for any
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta
,

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t)-\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t)\rVert_{F}

(F.23)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\Big(1+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}\bar{B}_{T})(1+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert^{2})

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)+(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta_{t,j})-\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\Big(1+\Delta tM^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}\bar{B}_{T})(1+\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}B_{T}(K\Delta t/2)(1+r+r^{2})(\Delta t/2)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}\bar{B}_{T})(1+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\Big(1+\Delta t\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}\bar{B}_{T})(1+r+r^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}\bar{B}_{T})B_{T}(K\Delta t^{2}/4)(1+r+r^{2})^{2}

Therefore, applying (
F.23
), we deduce that for any
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t,1
, we have:

\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq\sqrt{N+1}B_{T}(K\Delta t^{2}/4)(1+r+r^{2})\frac{\exp(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}\bar{B}_{T})(1+r+r^{2}))}{\Delta t}C_{1}L^{-1}

(F.24)

where
C_{1}:=\sqrt{N+1}B_{T}K(1+r+r^{2})\exp(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}\bar{B}_{T})(1+r+r^{2}))/4
.

Bounding
J_{2}
:

For any
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta,i\in[N+1],j\in[M]
, we have
\lVert g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})_{:,i}\rVert\leq B_{T}
. Hence, by the Hoeffding’s inequality, for any
z>0
we have

\mathbb{P}(\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})_{:,i}-\int_{\beta}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})_{:,i}\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert\geq z)\leq 2\exp(-\frac{z^{2}}{2B_{T}^{2}}M).

By the union bound over
i\in[N+1]
and
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta
, the above inequality implies

\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t}\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})-\int_{\beta}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\geq z)\leq 2(N+1)L\exp(-\frac{z^{2}}{2B_{T}^{2}}M).

(F.25)

We let
z=B_{T}\sqrt{2M^{-1}(\delta+\log((N+1)L))}
. Then, (
F.25
) turns into

\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t}\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})-\int_{\beta}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\geq B_{T}\sqrt{2M^{-1}(\delta+\log((N+1)L))}\leq\exp(-\delta).

(F.26)

Denote the event such that

\sup_{t}\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})-\int_{\beta}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B_{T}\sqrt{2M^{-1}(\delta+\log((N+1)L))}

by
E_{\delta}
. (
F.26
) directly indicates
\mathbb{P}(E_{\delta})\geq 1-\exp(-\delta)
.

Suppose that the high probability event
E_{\delta}
occurs. Let’s denote
B_{T}\sqrt{2M^{-1}(\delta+\log((N+1)L))}
by
B_{\delta}
for brevity. From Assumption
2
(iii), it follows that for any
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta
,

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t)\rVert_{F}\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

(F.27)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Delta t\lVert M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})-\int_{\beta}g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Delta t\lVert\int_{\beta}(g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})-g(\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t),\beta_{t,j}))\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}+\Delta t\sqrt{N+1}B_{\delta}+

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Delta t\int_{\beta}\lVert g(\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})-g(\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t),\beta_{t,j})\rVert_{F}\rho(\beta|t)d\beta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}(1+\Delta t\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+r+r^{2}))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Delta t\sqrt{N+1}B_{\delta}.

Repeatedly applying Equation (
F.27
) yields

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\bar{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{N+1}B_{\delta}}{\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+r+r^{2})}\exp(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+r+r^{2}))

(F.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{2}\sqrt{M^{-1}(\delta+\log((N+1)L))}.

for some constant
C_{2}>0
dependent on the parameters listed in the result.

Bounding
J_{3}
:

It’s worth noting that the convergence proof with a convergence rate of
O(\Delta t)=O(\frac{1}{L})
for
\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)
, the first-order Euler method for
T_{\rho}(H,t)
, is non-standard. This departure from convention arises because we do not assume the boundedness of the second-order derivative
\frac{d^{2}T_{\rho}(H,t)}{dt^{2}}
, instead relying on the continuity of
\rho(\cdot,t)
with respect to the depth index
t
. In this proof,
O(\cdot)
hides dependencies on
N
,
D
,
r
, and the parameters of the assumptions.

From the definition of
\widetilde{T}_{\rho}
and
T_{\rho}
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t)-T_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t)\rVert_{F}\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

(F.29)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t)-T_{\rho}(H,t)-\Delta t\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{F}}_{I_{1}}

\displaystyle+\Delta t\underbrace{\lVert\int_{\beta}(g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-g(\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t),\beta))\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{F}}_{I_{2}}.

To bound
I_{1}
, we use (
3.1
) to get

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{t}^{t+\Delta t}\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)\rho(\beta|s)d\beta ds-\Delta t\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{F}

(F.30)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta t\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}\lVert\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)\rho(\beta|s)d\beta-\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rho(\beta|t)d\beta\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta t\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}\lVert\int_{\beta}(g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)-g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta))\rho(\beta|s)d\beta\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle+\Delta t\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}\lVert\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)(\rho(\beta|s)-\rho(\beta|t))d\beta\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Delta t\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}\int_{\beta}\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)-g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\rho(\beta|s)d\beta

\displaystyle+\Delta t\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}\lVert\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)(\rho(\beta|s)-\rho(\beta|t))d\beta\rVert_{F}

Given that Proposition
C.1
establishes the Lipschitz continuity of
T_{\rho}(H,t)
with respect to
t
under the condition that
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
has a bounded support, we can conclude:

\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)-g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\leq C_{3,1}|t-s|\leq C_{3,1}\Delta t

(F.31)

for some constant
C_{3,1}>0
dependent on the parameters listed in the result. Furthermore, Lemma
C.1
and Proposition
C.1
demonstrate that
g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)
is both bounded and Lipschitz continuous with respect to
\beta
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}\lVert\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)(\rho(\beta|s)-\rho(\beta|t))d\beta\rVert_{F}=

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}\lVert\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)(\rho(\beta,s)-\rho(\beta,t))d\beta\rVert_{F}

(F.32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[t,t+\Delta t]}C_{3,2}\lVert\rho(\cdot,s)-\rho(\cdot,t)\rVert_{\mathrm{BL}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{3,2}C_{\rho}\Delta t

for some constant
C_{3,2}>0
dependent on the parameters listed in the result. Substituting (
F.31
) and (
F.32
) into (
F.30
), we find that there exists a constant
C_{3,5}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result such that
I_{1}\leq C_{3,5}\Delta t^{2}
.

Additionally, Assumption
2
(iii) implies that

\displaystyle I_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\beta}\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-g(\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\rho(\beta|t)d\beta

(F.33)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+r+r^{2})\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}

Therefore, by bounding
I_{1}+I_{2}
, we obtain the following inequality

\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t),\beta)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t+\Delta t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\leq C_{3,5}\Delta t^{2}+(1+C_{3,6}\Delta t)\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F},

(F.34)

which implies, after being used multiple times, that

\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)-\widetilde{T}_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\leq C_{3,5}\Delta t^{2}\frac{(1+C_{3,6}\Delta t)^{L+1}-1}{C_{3,6}\Delta t}\leq C_{3}L^{-1}

(F.35)

for any
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t,1
and some constant
C_{3}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result. Combining (
F.24
), (
F.28
), and (
F.35
) yields the desired result.
∎

F.7
Proof of Lemma
E.1

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\mu}(t)
be the measure induced by
T_{\rho}(H,t)
with
H\sim\mu
, and
\widetilde{\mu}
be
\widetilde{\mu}(t^{*})
. By verifying Lemma
C.1
, we establish that
\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B_{T}:=B\exp(K(1+r+r^{2}))
for any
H
and
t\in[0,1]
. Consequently,
\mathrm{supp}(\widetilde{\mu}(t))\subset\{T:\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B_{T}\}
for any
t\in[0,1]
. The remainder of the proof involves four steps:

Step I: Show that
p_{\rho}(H,t)
is Lipschitz continuous with respect to
(H,t)

In the proof of this proposition, when referring to the Lipschitz continuity of a function, we imply its Lipschitz continuity for
H
within the support of
\mu
.
Recall that we have shown in Lemma
C.3
that
p_{\rho}(H,t)
is universally bounded and Lipschitz continuous for
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{F}
and any
t\in[0,1]
.

From the formula of
p_{\rho}
in (
C.14
), for any
H,H^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)-p_{\rho}(H^{\prime},t)\rVert_{F}=\lVert\mathrm{vec}[p_{\rho}(H,t)]-\mathrm{vec}[p_{\rho}(H^{\prime},t)]\rVert

(F.36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\rho}(H^{\prime},1)]-(y(H)-y(H^{\prime}))|}_{I_{1}}\underbrace{\left\lVert\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)\right\rVert}_{I_{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H^{\prime},1)]-y(H^{\prime})|}_{I_{3}}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left\lVert\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)-\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H^{\prime},s),\beta)]\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)\right\rVert}_{I_{4}}.

From Proposition
C.1
, we observe that
p_{\rho}(H,t)
is Lipschitz continuous. Since
y(\cdot)
is
K_{y}
-Lipschitz continuous, as given in Assumption
4
(iii), we obtain that
I_{1}\lesssim\lVert H-H^{\prime}\rVert_{F}
. Moreover, from Assumption
1
and Lemma
C.1
, we see that
I_{3}
is universally bounded. Therefore, to show that
p_{\rho}(H,t)
is Lipschitz continuous for
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{F}
, it suffices to demonstrate that
I_{2}\lesssim 1
and
I_{4}\lesssim\lVert H-H^{\prime}\rVert_{F}
.

From Assumption
2
(iii), we have

I_{2}\leq\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\right\rVert\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)\leq\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+r+r^{2})\Big).

Thus, dividing
I_{4}
by the uniformly bounded part
I_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{4}\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\Big(\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H^{\prime},s),\beta)]-\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\Big)\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)-I_{\mathrm{div}\mathrm{vec}[T]}\right\rVert

(F.37)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\Big(\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H^{\prime},s),\beta)]-\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\Big)\right\rVert\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)-1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\phi_{TT}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T},r)\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H^{\prime},t)\rVert_{F}\Big)-1,

where the final inequality holds by Assumption
3
(iv).

By the Lipschitz continuity of
T_{\rho}(H,t)
for
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{F}
as stated in Proposition
C.1
, we have
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\rho}(H^{\prime},t)\rVert_{F}\lesssim\lVert H-H^{\prime}\rVert_{F}
. Hence, utilizing the universal boundedness of the last equation in (
F.37
), we derive
I_{4}\lesssim\lVert H-H^{\prime}\rVert_{F}
.

Considering all assertions regarding
I_{1},I_{2},I_{3}
and
I_{4}
, we conclude that
p_{\rho}(H,t)
is
C_{p}
-Lipschitz continuous with respect to
H
for some constant
C_{p}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result that is sufficiently large. The Lipschitz continuity of
p_{\rho}(H,t)
with respect to
t
could be easily derived from the boundedness of the Jacobian matrix, as asserted in Assumption
2
(iii). Moreover, since
p_{\rho}(H,t)
is universally bounded shown in Lemma
C.3
, we conclude that
p_{\rho}(H,t)
is Lipschitz continuous with respect to
(H,t)
for some universal Lipschitz constant
C_{p}
dependent on the parameters listed in the result that is sufficiently large.

Step II: Prepare bounds related to
p_{\rho}
for later use

(
C.14
) implies that
p_{\rho}
solves the adjoint equation

\mathrm{vec}[\dot{p}_{\rho}(H,t)]^{\top}=-\mathrm{vec}[p_{\rho}(H,t)]^{\top}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta

(F.38)

with

\left\lVert\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)]\rho(\beta,t)d\beta\right\rVert\leq(1+C_{\rho}/2)\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+r+r^{2})=:C_{0}

implied by Assumption
2
(iii). Therefore, the Grönwall’s inequality directly indicates that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}^{2}\geq\exp(-2C_{0}t)\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,1)\rVert_{F}^{2}\geq\exp(-2C_{0})\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)|^{2}]\geq 2\exp(-2C_{0})R(\rho),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\exp(2C_{0}t)\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,1)\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\exp(2C_{0})\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]-y(H)|^{2}]\leq 2\exp(2C_{0})R(\rho),

for any
t\in[0,1]
.

Step III: Construct the descent direction
\nu

By the well-posedness of the ODE solution to (
3.1
) as shown in Proposition
C.1
, the solution map
T_{\rho}(H,\cdot)
is invertible. Hence, for any
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
, there exists a continuous inverse map
T^{-1}_{t}
such that
T^{-1}_{t}(T_{\rho}(H,t))=H
for any
H
and
t\in[0,1]
. Let’s define the following function
\bar{F}(T)
to approximate

\bar{F}(T)=-p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})+\int_{\beta}g(T,\beta)\rho(\beta|t^{*})d\beta-\frac{h(T,w_{0})}{2},

The function
\bar{F}(T)
, arising from the composition of
p_{\rho}(\cdot,t)
and
T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(\cdot)
, exhibits continuity over
T\in\mathrm{supp}(\widetilde{\mu})
owing to the continuous nature of
p_{\rho}(\cdot,t)
and
T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(\cdot)
.
Therefore, Assumption
4
(ii) could be applied to
\bar{F}
. Since
f(\cdot,\theta)
is a universal kernel constrained on
\theta\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{1}}\times\pazocal{K}

[
52
]
, and
\bar{F}(T)
is continuous with respect to
T
, there exists a sequence
\{(c_{k},\theta^{k})\}_{k\geq 0}\subset(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}\theta_{1}}\times\pazocal{K})^{\mathbb{N}}
such that

\left\lVert\bar{F}(T)-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}c_{k}f(T,\theta^{k})\right\rVert_{\max}\leq\epsilon/3

(F.39)

given some
\epsilon>0
and any
T
such that
\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B_{T}
. Notably, since
f(T,\theta_{1}^{k},\theta_{2}^{k})=-f(T,-\theta_{1}^{k},\theta_{2}^{k})
, we could assume without loss of generality that
c^{k}\geq 0
for any
k
. Furthermore, there exists a constant
k_{\epsilon}
such that

\left\lVert\bar{F}(T)-\sum_{k=1}^{k_{\epsilon}}c_{k}f(T,{\theta}^{k})\right\rVert_{\max}\leq 2\epsilon/3.

(F.40)

We define
C(\epsilon):=\sum_{k=1}^{k_{\epsilon}}c_{k}
, and
\bar{\nu}(\beta)\in\pazocal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}\beta})
as the probability distribution such that, given
\bar{\beta}\sim\bar{\nu}
and for any
k\geq 0
,
\bar{\beta}
has probability
c^{k}/C(\epsilon)
of being
(2C(\epsilon)\theta_{1}^{k},\theta_{2},0)
. Then, (
F.40
) transforms into

\left\lVert F(T)-\int_{\beta}g(T,\beta)\bar{\nu}(\beta)d\beta\right\rVert_{\max}\leq 2\epsilon/3,

(F.41)

where

F(T)==-p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})+\int_{\beta}g(T,\beta)\rho(\beta|t^{*})d\beta.

From (
F.41
), we claim that there exists some
R(\epsilon)
such that

•

\bar{\nu}(\{\beta:1/R(\epsilon)\leq\lVert\beta\rVert\leq R(\epsilon)\})\geq 1/2
,

•

\left\lVert\int_{\lVert\beta\rVert>R(\epsilon)}g(T,\beta)\bar{\nu}(\beta)d\beta\right\rVert_{\max}\leq\epsilon/3.

We are now in the position to define the descent direction
\nu
. By defining
\nu\in\pazocal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}\beta})
as the measure obtained by truncating any part outside
1/R(\epsilon)\leq\lVert\beta\rVert\leq R(\epsilon)
from
\bar{\nu}
, and scaling the measure function by
1/\bar{\nu}(\{\beta:1/R(\epsilon)\leq\lVert\beta\rVert\leq R(\epsilon)\})\leq 2
, we can establish that

\left\lVert F(T)-\int_{\beta}g(T,\beta)\nu(\beta)d\beta\right\rVert_{\max}\leq\epsilon.

(F.42)

for any
T
such that
\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B_{T}
. A straightforward deduction from (
F.42
) is that for any
T
such that
\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B_{T}
, we have
\lVert F(T)-\int_{\beta}g(T,\beta)\nu(\beta)d\beta\rVert_{F}\leq\epsilon\sqrt{(N+1)D}
. It is clear that
\nu
has a bounded support as
\{\beta:1/R(\epsilon)\leq\lVert\beta\rVert\leq R(\epsilon)\}
. We will determine the value of
\epsilon
later, ensuring it based only on
N
,
D
,
r
, and the parameters of the assumptions.

Step IV: Upper bound
\int_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}(\beta,t^{*})\Big(\nu(\beta)-\rho(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta
to complete the proof

Utilizing the gradient definition in (
3.4
) and
\int_{\beta}g(T,\beta)\rho(\beta|t^{*})d\beta=F(T)+p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}(\beta,t^{*})\Big(\nu(\beta)-\rho(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\int_{\beta}\mathrm{Tr}\Big[g(T_{\rho}(H,t^{*}),\beta)^{\top}p_{\rho}(H,t^{*})\Big]\Big(\nu(\beta)-\rho(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta+\frac{\lambda}{2}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\Big(\nu(\beta)-\rho(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{T\sim\widetilde{\mu}(t)}\mathrm{Tr}\Big[g(T,\beta)^{\top}\Big(\widetilde{v}(\beta)-\rho(\beta|t^{*})\Big)p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})\Big]+\frac{\lambda}{2}(R(\epsilon)+r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{T\sim\widetilde{\mu}(t)}\mathrm{Tr}\Big[\Big(F(T)-\int_{\beta}g(T,\beta)\nu(\beta)d\beta\Big)^{\top}p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})\Big]}_{J_{1}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{T\sim\widetilde{\mu}(t)}\mathrm{Tr}\Big[p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})^{\top}p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})\Big]}_{J_{2}}+\frac{\lambda}{2}(R(\epsilon)+r)

For
\rho\in\pazocal{P}^{2}
concentrated on
P_{r}
, we observe

R(\rho)\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[\Big(|\mathrm{Read}[T_{\rho}(H,1)]|+|y(H)|\Big)^{2}]\leq\frac{1}{2}(B_{T}+B)^{2}.

Hence, to bound
J_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{T\sim\widetilde{\mu}(t)}\lVert F(T)-\int_{\beta}g(T,\beta)\nu(\beta)d\beta\rVert_{F}\cdot\lVert p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})\rVert_{F}dt

(F.43)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{(N+1)D}\epsilon\mathbb{E}_{T\sim\widetilde{\mu}(t)}\lVert p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})\rVert_{F}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(N+1)D\epsilon\Big(\mathbb{E}_{T\sim\widetilde{\mu}(t)}\lVert p_{\rho}(T^{-1}_{t^{*}}(T),t^{*})\rVert_{F}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(N+1)D\epsilon\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}(N+1)D\exp(C_{0})R(\rho)^{1/2}\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(N+1)(B_{T}+B)(N+1)D\exp(C_{0})R(\rho)\epsilon

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{3}R(\rho)\epsilon,

where
C_{3}:=(N+1)(B_{T}+B)(N+1)D\exp(C_{0})
.

To bound
J_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{T\sim\widetilde{\mu}(t)}\lVert p_{\rho}(T_{t}^{-1}(T),t)\rVert_{F}^{2}dt

(F.44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}^{2}dt

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\exp(-2C_{0})\lVert p_{\rho}(H,1)\rVert_{F}^{2}dt

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\exp(-2C_{0})R(\rho).

Combining (
F.43
) and (
F.44
), by choosing
\epsilon=\frac{1}{4}\exp(-2C_{0})/C_{3}
, which only depends on
N
,
D
,
r
, and the parameters of the assumptions, we have

\int_{\beta}\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}(\beta,t^{*})\Big(\nu(\beta)-\rho(\beta|t^{*})\Big)d\beta\leq-\frac{1}{4}\exp(-2C_{0})R(\rho)+\frac{\lambda}{2}\Big(R(\frac{1}{4}\exp(-2C_{0})/C_{3})+r\Big).

Setting
B_{r}=R(\frac{1}{4}\exp(-2C_{0})/C_{3}),C_{1}=\Big(R(\frac{1}{4}\exp(-2C_{0})/C_{3})+r\Big)/2
, and
C_{2}=\frac{1}{4}\exp(-2C_{0})
completes the proof.
∎

F.8
Proof of Lemma
C.1

Proof.

By applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we trivially obtain
\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}||\beta||_{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt\leq A
. Thus, leveraging (
3.1
) and Assumption
2
(i), we can infer

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq\lVert\dot{T}_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

\displaystyle=\lVert\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rho(\beta,t)d\beta\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

(F.45)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\beta}\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\beta}K(1+||\beta||_{2}+||\beta||_{2}^{2})\rho(\beta,t)\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}d\beta.

Therefore, by the Grönwall’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert T_{\rho}(H,0)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\exp(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}K(1+||\beta||_{2}+||\beta||_{2}^{2})\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt)

\displaystyle\leq\lVert H\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\exp(K(1+A+A^{2})).

∎

F.9
Proof of Lemma
C.2

Proof.

As per Lemma
C.1
, the boundedness of
\lVert T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
and
\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
is established by a constant
C:=\lVert H\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))>0
for all
t\in[0,1]
. Consequently, from (
3.1
), this implies

\displaystyle\lVert T_{\nu}(H,t_{1})-T_{\nu}(H,t_{2})\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\lVert\dot{T}_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

(F.46)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{\beta}\lVert g(T_{\nu}(H,t),\beta\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\nu(\beta,t)d\beta dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+C_{\rho}/2)KC(1+r+r^{2})(t_{2}-t_{1}).

for any
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,1]
.
Therefore,
T_{\nu}(H,t)
is
(1+C_{\rho}/2)KC(1+r+r^{2})
-Lipschitz with respect to
t
for
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
, and thus
\sqrt{N+1}(1+C_{\rho}/2)KC(1+r+r^{2})
-Lipschitz with respect to
t
for
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{F}
. Note that by (
3.1
),

\displaystyle\Delta(H,t):=

\displaystyle\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)-T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

(F.47)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{\top}\dot{T}_{\rho}(H,s)-\dot{T}_{\nu}(H,s)ds\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lVert\int_{0}^{\top}\int_{\beta}g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)\rho(\beta,t)d\beta ds-\int_{0}^{\top}\int_{\beta}g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)\nu(\beta,s)d\beta ds\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\top}\int_{\beta}\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)-g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)\rVert_{F}\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds}_{J_{1}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\lVert\int_{0}^{\top}\int_{\beta}g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)\Big(\rho-\nu\Big)(\beta,s)d\beta ds\rVert_{F}}_{J_{2}}

We then bound
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
using the following two lemmas separately. Firstly, since
\lVert T_{\rho}\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{N+1}C
and
\lVert T_{\nu}\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{N+1}C
, we have by Assumption
2
that

\displaystyle\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)-g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sup_{\lVert T\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{N+1}C}\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T,\beta)]\rVert_{2}\Big)\lVert T_{\rho}(H,s)-T_{\nu}(H,s)\rVert_{F}

(F.48)

\displaystyle\leq\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}C)(1+r+r^{2})\Delta(H,s)

Therefore, by (
F.48
) we have

J_{1}\leq\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}C)(1+C_{\rho}/2)(1+r+r^{2})\int_{0}^{\top}\Delta(H,s)ds.

(F.49)

Secondly, we aim to bound the integral
J_{2}
given Assumption
2
and
\lVert T_{\nu}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq C
on
t\in[0,1]
. Again by Assumption
2
we have

\lVert\nabla_{\beta}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)]\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{N+1}\lVert\nabla_{\beta}g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)_{:,i}\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}^{2}}\leq\sqrt{N+1}\phi_{P}(C)(1+r)

(F.50)

\lVert\nabla_{T}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)]\rVert_{F}\leq\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}C)(1+r+r^{2})

(F.51)

Since
T_{\nu}(H,s)
is
\sqrt{N+1}(1+C_{\rho}/2)KC(1+r+r^{2})
-Lipschitz with respect to
s
for
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{F}
(as shown in (
F.46
)), by (
F.51
), we obtain that
g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)
is
\sqrt{N+1}(1+C_{\rho}/2)KC\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}C)(1+r+r^{2})^{2}
-Lipschitz with respect to
s
. Thus,
g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)
is
C^{\prime}
-Lipschitz with respect to
(s,\beta)
, where
C^{\prime}=\sqrt{N+1}(1+C_{\rho}/2)KC\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}C)(1+r+r^{2})^{2}+\sqrt{N+1}\phi_{P}(C)(1+r)
. This indicates, by the Kantorovich-Rubinstein Theorem (see Theorem 5.10 of
[
68
]
, for example), that

J_{2}=\lVert\int_{0}^{1}\int_{D}g(T_{\nu}(H,s),\beta)\Big(\rho-\nu\Big)(\beta,s)d\beta ds\rVert_{F}\leq C^{\prime}W_{1}(\rho,\nu).

(F.52)

Define
C^{*}:=\max\{C^{\prime},\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}C)(1+r+r^{2})\}
. By combining (
F.49
) and (
F.52
), we have

\Delta(H,t)\leq C^{*}\int_{0}^{\top}\Delta(H,s)ds+C^{*}W_{1}(\rho,\nu).

(F.53)

Applying the Grönwall’s inequality then shows

\Delta(H,t)\leq C^{*}\exp(C^{*}t)W_{1}(\rho,\nu).

(F.54)

Specifically, we have
\lVert T_{\rho}(H,1)-T_{\nu}(H,1)\rVert_{F}\leq C^{*}\exp(C^{*})W_{1}(\rho,\nu)
.
∎

F.10
Proof of Lemma
C.3

Proof.

Let’s consider a fixed
(\beta,t)
pair within
P_{r}
.
Given Lemma
C.1
, we establish
\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))
. Consequently, under Assumption
1
, we deduce

\displaystyle\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{N+1}\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

(F.55)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{N+1}K\lVert T_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}(1+r+r^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))(1+r+r^{2}).

On the other hand, from (
C.14
), we have

\displaystyle\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq|\mathrm{Read}(T_{\rho}(H,1))-y(H)|\cdot\left\lVert\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds]\Big)\right\rVert_{2}

(F.56)

\displaystyle\leq(B+B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2})))\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\right\rVert\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq(B+B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2})))\exp\Big(\int_{t}^{1}\int_{\beta}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[g(T_{\rho}(H,s),\beta)]\right\rVert\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq(B+B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2})))\exp\Big(\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\phi_{T}(N,D,\lVert T_{\rho}(H,s)\rVert_{F})(1+\lVert\beta\rVert+\lVert\beta\rVert^{2})\rho(\beta,s)d\beta ds\Big)

\displaystyle\leq(B+B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2})))\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))(1+A+A^{2})\Big)

Combining (
F.55
) and (
F.56
), we could obtain

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{\delta Q}{\delta\rho}(\beta,t)\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}\lVert g(T_{\rho}(H,t),\beta)\rVert_{F}\lVert p_{\rho}(H,t)\rVert_{F}+\frac{\lambda}{2}r^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))(1+r+r^{2})(B+B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2})))

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))(1+A+A^{2})\Big)+\frac{\lambda}{2}r^{2}.

∎

F.11
Proof of Lemma
C.4

Proof.

By employing the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we readily observe that
\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta\rVert\leq A
. Consequently, leveraging (
2.4
) and Assumption
2
, we ascertain that for any
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\Big\{1+\frac{1}{2ML}\sum_{j=1}^{M}K(1+\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert^{2})\Big\}

(F.57)

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\Big\{1+\frac{1}{2ML}\sum_{j=1}^{M}K(1+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert^{2})\Big\}.

Therefore, by applying (
F.57
) multiple times, we obtain that for any
t=0,\Delta t/2,\dots,(L-1/2)\Delta t,1
,

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,0)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\prod_{t}\Big\{1+\frac{1}{2ML}\sum_{j=1}^{M}K(1+\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert\theta_{t,j}\rVert^{2})\Big\}\Big\{1+\frac{1}{2ML}\sum_{j=1}^{M}K(1+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert w_{t,j}\rVert^{2})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert H\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\exp\Big(\frac{K}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}1+\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert+\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert H\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\exp\Big(K(1+A+A^{2})\Big).

∎

F.12
Proof of Lemma
C.5

Proof.

Lemma
C.4
shows that
\lVert\widetilde{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
and
\lVert\widetilde{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
are bounded by
B_{T}:=B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))
for any
H
and
t\in[0,1]
.
From (
2.4
), for any
t=0,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t
, from Assumption
2
(ii) and (iii) we have

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}

(F.58)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}+\frac{\Delta t/2}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),\theta_{t,j})-f(\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t),\widetilde{\theta}_{t,j})\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}+(\Delta t/2)\sqrt{N+1}\phi_{P}(B_{T})(1+r)M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\theta_{t,j}-\widetilde{\theta}_{t,j}\rVert

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Delta t/2)\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})(1+r+r^{2})\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}(1+C_{1}(\Delta t/2))+(\Delta t/2)C_{2}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\theta_{t,j}-\widetilde{\theta}_{t,j}\rVert.

for some constant
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
depending only
N,D,r
and assumptions. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t)\rVert_{F}\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}(1+C_{1}(\Delta t/2))

(F.59)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Delta t/2)C_{2}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert w_{t,j}-\widetilde{w}_{t,j}\rVert.

Combining (
F.58
) and (
F.59
), we derive

\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t+\Delta t)\rVert_{F}\leq\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}(1+C_{3}\Delta t)+\Delta tC_{3}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta_{t,j}-\widetilde{\beta}_{t,j}\rVert.

(F.60)

where
C_{3}
is a constant depending solely on
N
,
D
,
r
, and the parameters of the assumptions. Iterating (
F.60
) multiple times yields

\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)-\widehat{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}(H,t)\rVert_{F}\leq\exp(C_{3})\frac{1}{ML}d(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})

for any
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,1
.
∎

F.13
Proof of Lemma
C.6

Proof.

By verifying that
\Theta
satisfies the conditions outlined in Lemma
C.4
, we establish
\lVert\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B\exp(K(1+A+A^{2}))
. The first two results stem from Assumption
2
(i) and (ii), with recognition that
\lVert T\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{N+1}\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}
for any
T\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times(N+1)}
.
As for the third result, consider
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t,1
, where

\displaystyle\max\{\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t)\rVert_{F},\lVert\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)\rVert_{F}\}

(F.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\{\lVert\mathrm{vec}[\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t)]\rVert,\lVert\mathrm{vec}[\widehat{p}_{\Theta}(H,t+\Delta t/2)]\rVert\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,1)]-y(H)|

\displaystyle\Big\{\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\left\lVert I_{\mathrm{dim}\theta}+(\Delta t/2)\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\left\lVert I_{\mathrm{dim}w}+(\Delta t/2)\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]\right\rVert\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathrm{Read}[\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,1)]-y(H)|

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]\\
j\in[M]\end{subarray}}\Big(1+(\Delta t/2)\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\right\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle\Big(1+(\Delta t/2)\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s+\Delta t/2),w_{s,j})]\right\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(B+B_{T})

\displaystyle\exp\Big((\Delta t/2)\sum_{(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[f(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,s),\theta_{s,j})]\right\rVert

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Delta t/2)\sum_{(s-t)/\Delta t+1\in[(1-t)/\Delta t]}M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[T]}\mathrm{vec}[h(\widehat{T}_{\Theta}(H,t),w_{s,j})]\right\rVert\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(B+B_{T})\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}B_{T})\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}(1+\lVert\beta\rVert+\lVert\beta\rVert^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(B+B_{T})\exp\Big(\phi_{T}(N,D,\sqrt{N+1}KB_{T})(1+A+A^{2})\Big),

where the first inequality arises from the fact that the matrix
2
-norm is greater equal than the norm of any of its columns, and the fourth inequality follows from Assumption
2
(iii).
∎

F.14
Proof of Lemma
C.7

Proof.

Note that
\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert\leq r
for any
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t
and
j\in[M]
with its expectation denoted as
\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert\rho(\beta,t)d\beta
. Applying Hoeffding’s inequality yields, for any
z>0
and
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t

\mathbb{P}(|M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert^{2}-\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta|\geq z)\leq 2\exp(-\frac{z^{2}}{2r^{2}}M).

By applying the union bound over
t=0,\Delta t,\dots,(L-1)\Delta t
, the inequality above implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|\frac{1}{ML}\sum_{t}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{L}\sum_{t}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta|\geq z)\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t}|M^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{M}\lVert\beta_{t,j}\rVert^{2}-\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta|\geq z)

(F.62)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2L\exp(-\frac{z^{2}}{2r^{2}}M).

In addition, we have

\displaystyle|\frac{1}{L}\sum_{t}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta dt|\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{|t-s|\leq\Delta t}|\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,t)d\beta-\int_{\beta}\lVert\beta\rVert^{2}\rho(\beta,s)d\beta|

(F.63)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r^{2}L^{-1}\sup_{|t-s|\leq\Delta t}\lVert\rho(\cdot,t)-\rho(\cdot,s)\rVert_{\mathrm{BL}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle r^{2}C_{\rho}L^{-1}.

Combining (
F.62
) and (
F.63
), and setting
z=r\sqrt{2M^{-1}(\delta+\log(2L))}
completes the proof.
∎

F.15
Proof of Lemma
C.8

Proof.

The proof will be trivial by noting the equality

\displaystyle\prod_{l=1}^{L}A_{l}-\prod_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}=\sum_{l=1}^{L}\Big(\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}B_{s}(A_{l}-B_{l})\prod_{s=l+1}^{L}A_{s}\Big).

Hence, we have

\lVert\prod_{l=1}^{L}A_{l}-\prod_{l=1}^{L}B_{l}\rVert\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}\lVert\prod_{s=1}^{l-1}B_{s}\rVert\cdot\lVert A_{l}-B_{l}\rVert\cdot\lVert\prod_{s=l+1}^{L}A_{s}\rVert\leq C\sum_{l=1}^{L}\lVert A_{l}-B_{l}\rVert.

∎

Appendix G
Assumption verification for a concrete example

In this section, we consider

f(Z,\theta)=VZ\mathrm{softmax}(Z^{\top}WZ)

(G.1)

with the collection of parameters
\theta=\mathrm{vec}[V,W],
where
\mathrm{softmax}
denotes the column-wise softmax function. Moreover, consider

h(Z,w)=W_{2}\mathrm{HuberizedReLU}(W_{1}H)

(G.2)

with the collection of parameters
w=\mathrm{vec}[W_{1},W_{2}].
Here,
\mathrm{HuberizedReLU}
denotes the entry-wise HuberizedReLU activation function defined as

\mathrm{HuberizedReLU}(x)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &0,&&\mathrm{if}\ z\leq 0;\\
&z^{2}/2,&&\mathrm{if}\ z\in[0,1];\\
&z-1/2,&&\mathrm{if}\ z\geq 1.\end{aligned}\right.

Then, we can consider a Transformer model defined by equations (
2.1
), (
2.2
), and (
2.4
) in the paper, where the functions
f
and
h
are specified above. We suppose that this Transformer model is applied to a learning task with data that satisfies Assumption
1
. We have the following proposition.

Proposition G.1
.

Consider the Transformer model defined by equations (
2.1
), (
2.2
), and (
2.4
), with
f(Z,\theta)
and
h(Z,w)
defined in (
G.1
) and (
G.2
) respectively. Then Assumptions
2
-
4
all hold.

Proof.

We omit the detailed derivations for the function
h(Z,w)
, which corresponds to the MLP part, in our verification of Assumptions
2
and
3
, as
h(Z,w)
satisfies Assumptions
2
and
3
is relatively more intuitive, especially given the proofs for
f(Z,\theta)
.

Denote
Z=(z_{1},\dots,z_{N+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times N+1}.
Then the function
f
can be rewritten as

f(Z,\theta)=VZ\mathrm{softmax}(Z^{\top}WZ)=(f(Z,\theta)_{:,i})_{1\leq i\leq N+1},

where

f(Z,\theta)_{:,i}=\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}P_{ij}Vz_{j}
and
P_{i,:}=\mathrm{softmax}(Z^{\top}Wz_{i}).
Next, we calculate the derivatives of
f(Z,\theta)_{:,i}
with respect to
Z
and
\theta
as follows:

For
Z
:
the Jacobian
J\in\mathbb{R}^{(N+1)D\times(N+1)D}
is
J=(J_{ij})_{1\leq i,j\leq N+1},
where
J_{ij}=\frac{\partial f_{:,i}}{\partial z_{j}}(Z,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}.
After calculation, we obtain

J_{ij}=VZQ_{i}\big[E_{ji}Z^{\top}W+Z^{\top}W^{\top}\delta_{ij}\big]+P_{ij}V,

where
Q_{i}:=\mathrm{diag}(P_{i:})-P^{\top}_{i:}P_{i:},

E_{ij}
is the matrix with zeros everywhere except one the
(i,j)
-th entry, and
\delta_{ij}
is the Kronecker delta (
1
if
i=j
,
0
otherwise).

For
\theta
:
Define
A_{i}=Z^{\top}Wz_{i}.
After calculation, we have

\frac{\partial P_{ij}}{\partial A_{kl}}=P_{ij}(\delta_{ik}-P_{il}),\quad\frac{\partial A_{ij}}{\partial W_{kl}}=Z_{ki}Z_{jl}

(G.3)

Thus, by the chain rule, we have

\nabla_{W_{kl}}f(Z,\theta)_{:,i}=\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}Z_{ki}Z_{jl}P_{ij}(\delta_{ik}-P_{il})Vz_{j}.

(G.4)

Moreover, we have

\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[V]}f(Z,\theta)_{:,i}=\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}P_{ij}(z_{j}^{\top},\dots,z_{j}^{\top})^{\top},

(G.5)

where
(z_{j}^{\top},\dots,z_{j}^{\top})^{\top}
contains
D
copies of
z_{j}
.
We then verify the assumptions one by one.

For Assumption
2
(i), we have

\displaystyle\lVert f(T,\theta)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}=\lVert VT\mathrm{softmax}(T^{\top}WT)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq

\displaystyle\lVert V\rVert_{2}\cdot\lVert T\rVert_{2}\cdot\lVert\mathrm{softmax}(T^{\top}WT)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}\cdot\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\cdot\lVert\mathrm{softmax}(T^{\top}WT)\rVert_{1-\mathrm{col}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}\cdot\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},

where the second-to-the-last inequality follows by the fact that
\ell_{2}
-norm can be upper bounded by the
\ell_{1}
-norm, and the last inequality follows by the fact that each column of the softmax output has an
\ell_{1}
-norm equaling one. Therefore, the first condition in Assumption
2
with
K=1
is verified for the function
f
in (
G.1
).

For
h
in (
G.2
), we have

\displaystyle\lVert h(T,w)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}=\lVert W_{2}\mathrm{HuberizedReLU}(W_{1}T)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq

\displaystyle\lVert W_{2}\rVert_{2}\cdot\lVert\mathrm{HuberizedReLU}(W_{1}T)\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert W_{2}\rVert_{2}\cdot\lVert W_{1}T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\cdot\lVert w\rVert_{2}^{2}\cdot\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}},

where the second inequality follows by the property of HuberizedReLU that
|\mathrm{HuberizedReLU}(x)|\leq|x|.
This demonstrates that Assumption
2
(i) with
K=1
holds for
h
in (
G.2
) as well.

For Assumption
2
(ii), (
G.5
) leads to

\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[V]}f(T,\theta)_{:,i}\rVert_{2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}\lVert P_{ij}T_{:,j}^{\top}\rVert_{2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}P_{ij}\lVert T_{:,j}\rVert_{2}\leq\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}.

Moreover, (
G.4
) leads to

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla_{\mathrm{vec}[W]}f(T,\theta)_{:,i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq k,l\leq D}\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}\lVert Z_{ki}Z_{jl}P_{ij}(\delta_{ik}-P_{il})VT_{:,j}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sum_{1\leq k,l\leq D}\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}P_{ij}\lVert T_{ki}T_{jl}VT_{:,j}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{1\leq j\leq N+1}\sum_{1\leq k,l\leq D}\lVert T_{ki}T_{jl}VT_{:,j}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\max_{1\leq j\leq N+1}\sum_{1\leq k,l\leq D}|T_{ki}T_{jl}|\lVert V\rVert_{2}\lVert T_{:,j}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}^{2}\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}

Combining the two equations above gives Assumption
2
(ii) with
\phi_{P}(\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}})=\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}+2\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}^{2}
.

For Assumption
2
(iii), we have

\lVert J\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq N+1}\lVert J_{ij}\rVert^{2}_{2}}\leq(N+1)\max_{1\leq i,j\leq N+1}\lVert J_{ij}\rVert_{2}.

For any
1\leq i,j\leq N+1,
we have

\displaystyle\lVert J_{ij}\rVert_{2}\leq

\displaystyle P_{ij}\lVert V\rVert_{2}+\lVert T\rVert_{2}\lVert Q_{i}\rVert_{2}\lVert E_{ji}T^{\top}W+T^{\top}W^{\top}\delta_{ij}\rVert_{2}\lVert V\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert V\rVert_{2}\Big(1+2\lVert T\rVert_{2}^{2}\lVert W\rVert_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}+2\lVert T\rVert_{F}^{2}\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+2\lVert T\rVert_{F}^{2})(1+\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}+\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{2}).

Hence, we have

\lVert J\rVert_{2}\leq 2N\lVert T\rVert_{F}^{2}\cdot(N+1)(1+2\lVert T\rVert_{F}^{2})(1+\lVert\theta\rVert_{2}+\lVert\theta\rVert^{2}_{2}).

The above equation demonstrates that for
f
, Assumption
2
(iii) holds with
\phi_{T}(N,D,\lVert T\rVert_{F})=(N+1)(1+2\lVert T\rVert_{F}^{2})
.
We have verified Assumption
2
for the attention layer encoder
f
. The verification for
h
is similar and easier.

Next, we verify Assumption
3
. Given that we are currently considering the example where the encoder employs a smooth univariate activation function, we can prove stronger results by removing the expectation
\mathbb{E}_{\mu}
.

(i) and (iii): Given the calculation of derivatives in (
G.4
) and (
G.5
) we have presented above, we first show that

P_{ij}=\mathrm{softmax}(Z^{\top}Wz_{i})

is locally Lipschitz continuous with respect to
Z
and
\theta
. By (
G.3
) and the chain rule, we can derive that

\nabla_{W_{kl}}P_{ij}=\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}Z_{ki}Z_{jl}P_{ij}(\delta_{ik}-P_{il}),\quad\frac{\partial P_{ij}}{\partial z_{j}}=Q_{i}\big[E_{ji}z_{j}^{\top}W+z_{j}^{\top}W^{\top}\delta_{ij}\big].

and the local Lipschitz continuity is then obvious given the boundedness of
\nabla_{W_{kl}}P_{ij}
and
\frac{\partial P_{ij}}{\partial z_{j}}
with respect to necessary parameters.

As we prove that
P_{ij}
is locally Lipschitz continuous, given that then each component in (
G.4
) and (
G.5
) is locally Lipschitz continuity with respect to both
Z
and
\theta
, and is obviously bounded by an increasing function of
N,D,\lVert\theta\rVert,K_{T},L_{T}
. Then the local Lipschitz continuity is straightforward as they are all sufficiently smooth.

(ii) and (iv): Because the norm of the difference of two Jacobian matrices
\lVert J^{1}-J^{2}\rVert_{2}
is bounded by
\sqrt{\sum_{1\leq i,j\leq N+1}\lVert J^{1}_{ij}-J^{2}_{ij}\rVert_{2}^{2}},
it suffices to show that
J_{ij}
is locally Lipschitz continuous with respect to both
\theta
and
Z.
Again each component of
J_{ij}
that depends on
Z
or
\theta,
i.e.
Z,Q_{i},W,P_{ij},
is bounded by an increasing function of
N,D,K_{P},L_{T},K_{T},\lVert\theta\rVert,
and is locally Lipschitz continuous given sufficient smoothness. Hence, (ii) and (iv) also hold.

For Assumption
4
, we consider the pair
(g,\alpha)=(h,w),
and the partition
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})
with
\alpha_{1}=W_{2},

\alpha_{2}=W_{1}
. We also let a compact set
\pazocal{K}=\{W_{1}:\lVert W_{1}\rVert\leq 1\}
. Then Assumption
4
(i) on the partial
1
-homogeneity property straightforwardly holds:

h(T,W_{1},c\cdot W_{2})=c\cdot W_{2}\mathrm{HuberizedReLU}(W_{1}H)=c\cdot h(T,W_{1},W_{2}).

Regarding Assumption
4
(ii) on the universal kernel property, we first note that according to the choice
(g,\alpha)=(h,w)
, this assumption is purely an assumption on the MLP part of the Transformer. Here we give the detailed proof as follows.

First of all, according to the classic universal approximation theory (see the wiki page of “universal approximation theorem” and
[
34
,
18
,
57
]
for more details), we know that two-layer fully-connected networks with non-polynomial activation functions and without any constraints on its parameters are universal approximates.

Therefore, we know that the function class

\mathrm{span}\{W_{2}\mathrm{ReLU}^{2}(W_{1}T):W_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}(W_{1})},W_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}(W_{2})}\}

is dense in
\pazocal{C}(\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B,\mathbb{R}^{D\times(N+1)})
. Moreover, by the definition of HuberizedReLU, for any
B>0
and any
\widehat{W}_{1}
,
\widehat{W}_{2}
, there exist small constant
c
such that
c\cdot\widehat{W}_{1}\in\pazocal{K}
,
c\cdot\lVert\widehat{W}\rVert_{1}\leq B^{-1}
, and

\displaystyle c^{-2}\cdot\widehat{W}_{2}\mathrm{HyberizedReLU}(c\cdot\widehat{W}_{1}T)=

\displaystyle c^{-2}\cdot\widehat{W}_{2}\mathrm{ReLU}^{2}(c\cdot\widehat{W}_{1}T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c^{2}\cdot c^{-2}\cdot\widehat{W}_{2}\mathrm{ReLU}^{2}(\widehat{W}_{1}T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{2}\mathrm{ReLU}^{2}(\widehat{W}_{1}T),

where the second equation follows by the positive
2
-homogeneity of
\mathrm{ReLU}^{2}
activation. This implies that

\{W_{2}\mathrm{ReLU}^{2}(W_{1}T):W_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}(W_{1})},W_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}(W_{2})}\}\subseteq\{W_{2}\mathrm{HyberizedReLU}(W_{1}T):W_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}(W_{2})}\times\pazocal{K}\}.

Therefore, we conclude that
\mathrm{span}\{W_{2}\mathrm{HyberizedReLU}(W_{1}T):W_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\mathrm{dim}(W_{2})}\times\pazocal{K}\}
is dense in
\pazocal{C}(\lVert T\rVert_{2-\mathrm{col}}\leq B,\mathbb{R}^{D\times(N+1)})
. This finishes the validation of Assumption
4
.
∎

Appendix H
Experiments

As discussed in Sections
3
and
4
, our mean-field approximation results and global convergence results are asymptotic guarantees requiring exponentially large number of heads
M
and number of layers
L
. Such results are due to the nature of mean-field type analysis. In practice, we frequently observe that global convergence can be achieved by Transformer models of reasonable sizes. In this section, we run simple experiments on training Vision Transformers (ViT)
[
24
]
on the CIFAR-10 datasets to demonstrate global convergence in practical applications.

We train Vision Transformers with different numbers of heads and layers. In all our experiments, we split each CIFAR-10 image into four patches and then pass the patches into Vision Transformer models. We keep the dimension of each attention head to be
128
. The output of each self-attention layer is passed through a single-hidden-layer feedforward component with 128 hidden neurons and GeLU activation. Both the self-attention and feedforward components include skip connections. We implement dropout in the self-attention layers as well as the feedforward layers with a dropout probability of 0.1. The model is attached to a linear classifier.

In all experiments, we train the ViT models using Adam for 200 epochs with a mini-batch size
512
. We set the initial learning rate to be
1e-4
, and implement a cosine annealing learning rate schedule. We do not use any data augmentation or explicit regularization techniques, so that global convergence for large enough models implies close-to-zero training loss and close to
100\%
training accuracy.

In the first set of experiments, we fix the depth of the ViT to 6 layers (i.e., there are six self-attention layers, each followed by a single-hidden-layer feedforward component). We train such Vision Transformers with the numbers of heads per layer ranging from
4
to
40
, and record the training loss and training accuracy throughout training. The results are given in Figure
1
. Based on the results, it is clear that for ViT models with more than
20
heads can achieve close-to-zero training loss and close to
100\%
training accuracy, demonstrating global convergence on the CIFAR-10 training data.

In the second set of experiments, we fix number of heads in the ViT model per layer to 8. We train such Vision Transformers with depths ranging from
2
to
20
, and record the training loss and training accuracy throughout training. The results are given in Figure
2
. Again, the results indicate that ViT models with more than
16
layers can achieve close-to-zero training loss and close to
100\%
training accuracy on the CIFAR-10 dataset, implying global convergence.

We note that all these experiments are conducted on a standard GPU card. We can observe clear global convergence when the Vision Transformer is sufficiently wide or deep, but still within reasonable scales. This indicates that, although our theoretical guarantees require extremely large numbers of heads and layers due to the limitations of the mean-field technical tools, global convergence can be achieved by Transformers of reasonable sizes in practice.
